# STYLE-0 — THE STYLE AUDIT OF EVERY PROSE SOURCE AGAINST THE OWNER'S LAW (2026-11-07)

**THE BALANCE (WIRING-RULINGS §7, the line every row below is judged by): PLAIN AND CONCRETE, NEVER PLAIN AND GENERIC; vivid only where the town's own facts make it vivid; flavour in the intro positions (the cover line, the Overview's first paragraph, the crisis text, the Tonight line, a section's lead), description everywhere else.**

Seat: Opus 5.5, child of the cloud chair (session `01FE9mEvGxnFx6WTBkEC6ie9`). READ-ONLY on product, annex and registers: this file is the only byte the lane writes. Base: `lane/rung2-landing-2026-11-06` @ `f5dc50c6b` (the composed census annex). Law: `WIRING-RULINGS-2026-11-07.md` §4–§8 on `origin/lane/wiring-inventory-2026-11-07` @ `648e0632a`, read whole.

## §0 Method (what was measured, and how)

- **Evidence.** `node scripts/audit/soak-review.mjs --count 12 --workers 2` (exit 0, 12 settlements, 0 errors) — four dossiers read as they print: thorp `soak1k-0` (Albamunicipium, mass migration) read WHOLE (screen, PDF text, Foundry journal); village `soak1k-2` (Copanucan, occupation), town `soak1k-3` (Al-karez, famine), city `soak1k-4` (Portaminus, raiders + migration) read whole on the screen and by section in the PDF/Foundry parts. Then **the composed 1,000** on this branch: `--count 1000 --workers 4` (exit 0, 1,000 settlements, 0 errors, 173 s) into scratch — **no 1,000 run existed on the branch**, so the chair's referee was run here; its `aggregates/repetition-prose.json` is the baseline in §1c. Neither run is committed (scratch only; no artifact write).
- **The annex corpus** was read from its projections (`src/data/dossierStateProse/*.generated.js` — what prints — and `src/data/dossierCausalProse.generated.js`): **2,810 variants** (state 2,342 across 6 desks · causal 468), every variant one row, each flagged `lit` when all its fixed fragments (≥12 chars between slots) occur in one of the 12 sample dossiers — **140 lit** (a LOWER bound: a variant whose fixed text is split by many slots under-matches; e.g. DS-DEF-3 v1 "The ordinary safety of {settlement} has stopped applying" reads 0 by the fragment test and printed in 7 of 12).
- **Six section auditors** (Opus subagents of this seat, one shared brief, every row judged, instructed to declare nothing their block does not READ) produced the §2b tables; this seat wrote §1c, §2a (the forty, three vantages each per §8), §3 and §4 directly from the dossiers, and checked the auditors' rows as §1 records.
- **Reason codes:** `aphorism` · `mannered` · `mechanism-first` · `machinery-leak` · `no-evidence` · `duplicate-surface`. **F/S:** `FRAME` (the rewrite repeats verbatim across towns) or `SLOTTED` (carries this town's own nouns). `needs` names a fact the better line wants that the pool does not declare — never written into the rewrite.

## §1 The counts

**Sources:** 2 authored annexes, each read through its 7 projections. There are 53 code-side writer files, all named in the §2b rows. Eleven of them were not on the brief's list:
- `src/domain/crisis/crisisCast.js`, `crisisHooks.js` and `crisisPage.js`: the Tonight and crisis-tab casting lines.
- `src/generators/factionDynamics.js`: the faction-web relation lines.
- `src/domain/display/threatAssessment.js`: the PDF threat rows.
- `src/generators/recordBatteryFindings.js`.
- `src/foundry/journalPages.js`.
- `src/copy/sectionHelp.js`: the section help lines that print as body text ("Bars show…", "As judged at the first survey").
- `src/domain/dossier/faithReading.js`, `powerStrata.js` and `hookLines.js`.

`src/data/cultureProfiles.js` and `npcData.js` are identity text and were audited for machinery only.

**Lines audited: 4,140.** 2,810 are annex variants, every one of them. 1,330 are code-side lines. **PASS 2,378 · REWRITE 1,762.** 401 of the REWRITE lines printed in the 12-town sample, and those are the ones that matter now. The rest are dark lines that print later or only in a campaign.

Power and defence counted every code a row carries; the other four counted the primary code only. So the by-reason sums run about 6% above 1,762, and they rank the reasons rather than partition the rows.

**The annex alone:** 2,810 variants, 1,121 REWRITE, of which 205 are causal and all dark. The rest are code-side.

**What this file can and cannot vouch for:**
- **CONFIRMED:** the counts, the tic and repetition measurements, the forty, §3 and §4. This seat measured or read each of them against the printed dossiers.
- **PLAUSIBLE:** the per-row codes and rewrites in §2b. Each is one auditor's judgment, made against its block's declaration. This seat checked them two ways:
  - **Mechanically:** every one of the 1,233 annex rows was checked against its block's declared slots. One rewrite uses a slot its block does not declare: DS-POW-1 / PROSPERITY adverse / v01 uses `{good}`. That slot is struck.
  - **By hand:** 15 rows were read against their blocks. Three of the 15 add a fact that no slot or declaration holds: DS-GEN-6 / isolated / v04 ("bread here costs what meat costs elsewhere"), DS-POP-1 / nobody / v01 ("every house lived in last year is lived in now") and JF-CPL-5a / v02 ("the carts go out on the same day each season").
  - **What follows for the lanes:** about one §2b rewrite in five may carry such a clause. So **a lane reads every §2b rewrite against its block's READS before taking it, and strikes any clause that states something the record does not hold.** The census law governs, and the rewrite is a proposal.
- **Vantage tags in §2b:** these were assigned by keyword and then hand-corrected in part. They are PLAUSIBLE, and the lane re-reads each against the block's READS.
- **`lit` counts:** these are a lower bound. The fragment matcher misses lines where the tier word replaces "town" and lines the composer cuts at the first clause.

**Coupling the lanes must carry:** `src/domain/coherence/crisisBlindFindings.js :: CLAIMS` quotes three economy rows by regex: food SECURE v3, income one-source v3 and export established v1. Rewriting any of those rows means changing its regex in the same cause.

### §1b The tics, measured (annex variants carrying the tic · hits in the 12 printed screen dossiers · towns of 12)

| tic | annex variants | hits in 12 | towns |
|---|---|---|---|
| "A stranger …" (as subject/opener) | 189 | 64 | 12 |
| "… rather than …" (the balanced contrast) | 295 | 61 | 12 |
| "arithmetic" | 30 | 18 | 11 |
| "the record" (engine word in a sentence) | 38 | 9 | 6 |
| "… are two different things" / "do not contradict" | 2 | 6 | 5 |
| "not merely" / ": not X, but Y" | 4 | 6 | 6 |
| "is a separate question from" | 2 | 5 | 5 |
| "going on being a {tier} while it does" | 1 | 5 | 5 |
| "nothing has broken yet" | 2 | 6 | 5 |
| "which is the kind of …" | 1 | 3 | 3 |

**Reading (CONFIRMED on the 12):** a printed screen dossier carries about **five "a stranger" sentences and five "rather than" balances**. Each is harmless once; across a page it is the tic the owner's §7(1) names ("noticed by the third town" — here it is noticed by the third section). The "a stranger" device is the annex's way of doing the owner's rule (1) — *show what a visitor notices* — but most of the 189 variants then state a judgment rather than a thing seen ("A stranger at X sees a serious perimeter and a serious absence of anyone standing in it"). The cure the rewrites apply: keep the visitor's EYE, drop the word "stranger", and name the thing seen.

**What the referee means for the rewrites.** Eleven of the twenty are FRAMES with no town noun at all (#3–#6, #10–#13, #16–#19 — the safety-profile and criminal-structure tables); every rewrite proposed for them in §2 is SLOTTED (the town's watch, wall, court or gang by name) or splits the frame into per-state variants. A SLOTTED rewrite lowers the exact-sentence repeat by construction (the slot differs per town); the skeleton repeat (`@`-normalized) falls only where the rewrite also varies by state — the rows say `+variants` where that is the cure. No row proposes a new fixed sentence for a pool already in this table.

## §2 THE REWRITE TABLES

### §2a THE FORTY FIRST-IMPRESSION LINES — for the chair to rule FIRST

Positions: C = the cover and PDF section leads · O = the Overview first screen · L = each section's lead · X = the crisis text · T = the Tonight sheet. "towns" = towns of the 1,000 that print the frame (`—` = not in the top list).

Each line gets three variants (WIRING-RULINGS §8). Each variant is written from one vantage — traveller, official, local, newcomer, trade or watch — and is tagged `[vantage · S|F]`. All three variants of a row declare the same facts. The seed picks one per town. The section lane writes the fourth.

Three of the forty are the chair's to rule as a class rather than a line: **#28/#14/#31 want the stress type in the pool key** (one variant set per crisis — a declaration change to DS-DEF-3, DS-CND-1, DS-DEF-8, not a sentence change); **#1/#8/#19/#20/#29/#30/#38/#39 wait on R-W7(e)'s `stress[].direction`** (W0) — the rewrite is written for arrivals and is refused on a departure record until the field exists; **#10 and #23 are record contradictions** (a port reason without a wharf; clerks in a thorp), which a rewrite cannot cure without the gate.

### §2b THE SECTION TABLES (every REWRITE row, by section, then block/pool)

#### §2b-1 ECONOMY

##### Counts
lines audited: 373 (346 annex + 27 code) · PASS: 225 · REWRITE: 148 · aphorism 18 · mannered 69 · mechanism-first 34 · machinery-leak 23 · no-evidence 4 · duplicate-surface 0 primary (5 secondary) · lit-in-sample REWRITE: 47 (27 annex + 20 code)

Reason counts use the primary code only. Lit status comes from my own recount over the 12 samples, matching the tier noun (town/thorp/hamlet/village/city/metropolis). The TSV's `sampleTowns` column undercounts any line containing "town", because "town" prints as the tier word. For example, DS-ECO-12 one-source v2 is 0 in the TSV and printed in 4 of the 12 samples.

| DS-ECO-5 / IMPAIRED / v3 | {settlement}'s {chain} produces less with every turn it goes without {resource}, and the trades below it are shortening their own work to match. | machinery-leak | S | trade | {settlement}'s {chain} makes less every month it goes without {resource}, and the trades that rely on it are cutting back too. | "turn" = engine tick |
| DS-ECO-5 / RUNNING / v2 | A stranger watching {settlement}'s {chain} sees the ordinary version of it: {resource} in at one end, {good} out at the other, and no gap between. | mannered | S | trade | {resource} goes into {institution} at {settlement} and comes out as {good}, with no gap in between. | |
| DS-ECO-5 / MAGIC large / v1 | The {chain} runs almost whole on a working. The books show the output nearly where it belongs and the line beneath it replaced rather than repaired. | mechanism-first | S | trade | {settlement}'s {chain} runs almost as it should, but on a working, not on its own supply. | |
| DS-ECO-5 / MAGIC large / v2 | A stranger would see nothing wrong with this {chain}. What is wrong is that the thing carrying it is a craft and not a road. | mannered, aphorism | S | trade | Nothing looks wrong with {settlement}'s {chain}. Its supply is gone, and a working is carrying it. | |
| DS-SUP-1 / PROCESSING HOUSE MISSING / v2 | A stranger looking for {institution} here will not find one. What the town takes out of the ground, it sends on for somebody else to finish. | mannered | S | trade | There is no {institution} in {settlement}. What the town takes out of the ground, it sends away for others to finish. | |
| DS-SUP-1 / UPSTREAM COVERED BY IMPORT / v2 | The {chain} is whole on the town's side and borrowed on the far side; {institution} has everything it needs except the beginning of the line. | mannered | S | trade | {institution} at {settlement} has everything it needs except the {resource}, which the town has to buy in. | |
| DS-SUP-1 / ENTREPÔT PASS-THROUGH / v1 | Nothing in this {chain} begins or ends here. Goods arrive finished, are counted, and go on, and the town's return is on the counting. | mannered | S | trade | Nothing in this {chain} is made at {settlement}. Goods arrive finished, are counted and go on, and the town is paid for the handling. | "in the counting" is repeated almost word for word in DS-ECO-12 entrepot v1 |
| DS-SUP-1 / ENTREPÔT PASS-THROUGH / v2 | A stranger tracing {good} through {settlement} finds no workshop in the line at all: only a yard, a clerk, and the next road. | mannered | S | traveller | Follow {good} through {settlement} and you find no workshop at all: only a yard, a clerk, and the next road. | |
| DS-SUP-1 / BLOCKED / v1 | The {chain} is on the rolls and off the road. There is no way to move what it makes, so it is not being made. | mannered | S | trade | {settlement} has no way to move what its {chain} makes, so nothing is being made. | |
| DS-ECO-6 / TIER ≥30 / v3 | A stranger who trades honestly here will be undersold and will not immediately understand why. {faction} is not paying what he is paying. | mannered | S | trade | Honest traders in {settlement} are undersold and cannot see why: {faction} pays no duty. | |
| DS-ECO-6 / TIER ≥15 / v0 | Significant off-book activity. Merchants operating in the shadow economy have a cost advantage over those paying duties and guild fees. | mechanism-first | S | trade | A good part of {settlement}'s trade goes unrecorded, and merchants who pay duties and guild fees are being undersold by those who do not. | canonical-at-zero (R-DST-C), owner's veto. Also EconomicsTab `scaleNote` |
| DS-ECO-6 / TIER ≥3 / v0 | Minor shadow activity. Petty theft and small-scale unlicensed trade. An inconvenience, not a structural threat. | mechanism-first | S | local | There is some petty theft in {settlement} and some trading without a licence. It is a nuisance, nothing more. | canonical-at-zero, owner's veto. Also EconomicsTab `scaleNote` |
| DS-ECO-7 / TALLIES / v0 | Events recorded after this settlement's last survey may not be fully counted in these tallies yet. | machinery-leak | F | official | These figures were taken some time ago. Anything that has happened since may not show in them yet. | frozen copy unit, owner's veto. Keeps "may", no em dash |
| DS-ECO-7 / TALLIES / v1 | The counts on this page were taken at the last full survey of {settlement}. Anything recorded since then may not have reached them yet. | machinery-leak | S | official | These figures for {settlement} were taken some time ago, and anything since may not show in them yet. | |
| DS-ECO-7 / TALLIES / v2 | These figures describe {settlement} as it was judged at its last survey. Events entered after that judgement may not be counted in them yet. | machinery-leak | S | official | These figures show {settlement} as it was when they were taken. Later events may not show in them yet. | |
| DS-ECO-7 / CATALOG / v0 | Events recorded after this settlement's last survey may not be fully counted in this catalog yet. | machinery-leak | F | official | This list was drawn up some time ago. A house opened or closed since may not show on it yet. | frozen copy unit, owner's veto |
| DS-ECO-7 / CATALOG / v1 | This list of what {settlement} offers was drawn at the last survey. A house opened or closed since then may not appear on it yet. | machinery-leak | S | official | This list of what {settlement} offers was drawn up some time ago. A house opened or closed since may not appear on it yet. | |
| DS-ECO-7 / CATALOG / v2 | The catalog describes the services {settlement} was judged to hold. Later changes may not be counted in it yet. | machinery-leak | S | official | This list shows the services {settlement} had when it was made. Later changes may not show on it yet. | |
| DS-ECO-7 / CATALOG / v3 | What the town could do for a visitor was written down at the last survey, and may have moved since. | machinery-leak | S | official | What {settlement} could do for a visitor was written down some time ago, and may have changed since. | |
| DS-ECO-8 / STRUGGLING / v2 | The books never quite close. What the village needs and what the village has are near enough to argue about and never near enough to settle. | aphorism | S | local | {settlement} never quite makes ends meet. Every month something the town needs waits for money that does not come. | |
| DS-ECO-8 / STRUGGLING / v3 | A stranger sees the shortage in the small things: a repair not made, a stall not restocked, a hall not heated. | mannered | S | local | The shortage at {settlement} shows in small things: a repair not made, a stall not restocked, a hall not heated. | the content is model-grade; only the opener is the tic |
| DS-ECO-8 / POOR / v3 | Lundey looks maintained rather than made. What stands is kept standing; nothing new goes up. | mannered | S | local | In {settlement} the old buildings are patched and kept standing, and nothing new goes up. | |
| DS-ECO-8 / MODERATE / v1 | The accounts come out steady with a narrow margin. It is an ordinary city's arithmetic and it works. | mannered | S | official | {settlement}'s accounts come out steady with a narrow margin. It is enough, and no more. | |
| DS-ECO-8 / PROSPEROUS / v1 | The surplus is large enough to be spent rather than merely held, and the spending shows in what the town keeps up. | no-evidence | S | official | {settlement} has money to spend and spends it on its halls and its defences. | |
| DS-ECO-8 / WEALTHY / v1 | {settlement} holds reserves deep enough to sit out most of what could happen to it. The accounts are not merely positive; they are cushioned. | mannered | S | official | {settlement} has reserves deep enough to ride out most of what could happen to it. | |
| DS-ECO-9 / SURPLUS / v3 | A stranger will be fed generously and will not be thanked for noticing. There is more than enough and it is unremarkable to anyone local. | mannered | S | traveller | Visitors to {settlement} are fed generously, and nobody local thinks it worth remarking. | |
| DS-ECO-9 / SECURE / v3 | Lundey is fed and only just comfortably. The margin is real and thin enough that a good season and a poor one feel different. | mechanism-first | S | local | {settlement} is fed, but only just. A poor harvest is felt at every table; a good one means a little put by. | Lit in 5 of 12. CLAIMS regex `fed and only just comfortably` must move with it |
| DS-ECO-9 / PRESSURED / v1 | The food counts come up short by a little, and the little has to be found somewhere every season. | mechanism-first | S | local | {settlement} grows a little less food than it needs, and every season the difference has to be bought, begged or done without. | |
| DS-ECO-9 / PRESSURED / v2 | There is enough, mostly. People here have started noticing the word "mostly". | mannered | S | local | There is enough food in {settlement}, mostly. Portions have got smaller, and people have noticed. | |
| DS-ECO-9 / DEFICIT / v1 | {settlement} cannot feed itself and does not come close. The shortfall is large, standing, and covered incompletely. | mechanism-first | S | local | {settlement} grows far less food than it needs, and not enough comes in to make up the rest. | |
| DS-ECO-9 / ACTIVE FAMINE / v1 | There is not enough food in {settlement} and no arrangement is bringing enough. The counts have stopped being a margin and started being a total. | aphorism | S | local | There is not enough food in {settlement} and nothing is bringing enough in. People are going hungry now. | |
| DS-ECO-9 / BLOCKADED / v2 | Nothing is coming in. The granary is the whole of the argument now and everybody is doing the same arithmetic. | mannered | S | watch | Nothing is coming in. Everyone in {settlement} talks about the granary and how many weeks it holds. | |
| DS-SUP-2 / STABLE / v3 | Nothing about {settlement}'s {chain} draws attention, which, in a line of supply, means all of it is arriving. | aphorism | S | trade | Nothing about {settlement}'s {chain} draws attention: everything it needs is arriving. | |
| DS-SUP-2 / SCARCE / v3 | Output is falling away rather than stopping. Each turn takes a little more off what the line delivers. | machinery-leak | S | trade | {settlement}'s {chain} is slowing, not stopping. Each month it delivers a little less. | "turn" = engine tick |
| DS-SUP-2 / BLOCKED / v3 | A stranger will find {institution} shut and the yard behind it empty. Whatever fed this place stopped feeding it. | mannered | S | trade | {institution} at {settlement} is shut and the yard behind it is empty. Whatever fed it has stopped. | |
| DS-ECO-10 / POSTURE none / v2 | Nothing leaves here for trade. A stranger looking to buy in quantity has come to the wrong town. | mannered | S | trade | Nothing leaves {settlement} for trade. A merchant who came to buy in quantity has come to the wrong place. | |
| DS-ECO-10 / POSTURE vulnerable / v3 | The trade exists at the sufferance of the approaches. Nothing is wrong with the goods; everything is uncertain about the going. | aphorism | S | trade | {settlement} has goods to sell, but the way out is unreliable, and a trader never knows whether a load will get through. | Avoid {access} here: `vulnerable` is forced by `isolated` |
| DS-ECO-10 / POSTURE established / v1 | Albamunicipium sells several things outward and depends on none of them alone. The export column is broad enough to lose a line without losing the trade. | mechanism-first | S | trade | {settlement} sends out {good} and several other goods besides; if one trade fails, the others still go. | CLAIMS regex `sells several things outward…` must move with it. Lit in 4 of 12, including a food-deficit thorp |
| DS-ECO-10 / SCARCITY shortage / v1 | The stores of {good} are well under what the market keeps, and the reckoning has climbed accordingly. | mannered | S | trade | {good} is scarce in {settlement} and its price has risen. | |
| DS-ECO-10 / SCARCITY shortage / v2 | There is little {good} to be had here, and it costs what little things cost. | mannered | S | trade | There is little {good} to be had in {settlement}, and what there is costs dear. | |
| DS-ECO-10 / SCARCITY shortage / v3 | A stranger wanting {good} in {settlement} will find it and will pay for the finding. | mannered | S | trade | {good} can still be found in {settlement}, at a price. | |
| DS-ECO-10 / SCARCITY surplus / v1 | {settlement} holds more {good} than it keeps room for, and the reckoning has come down under the weight of it. | mannered | S | trade | {settlement} has more {good} than it can store, and the price has fallen. | |
| DS-ECO-10 / SCARCITY surplus / v3 | A stranger stocking {good} does well here. The town has more than it can use. | mannered | S | trade | {good} is cheap in {settlement}; the town has more than it can use. | |
| DS-ECO-10 / FRESHNESS CAVEAT / v1 | The tallies below are the last survey's. They describe what {settlement} was judged to hold and may not carry what has happened since. | machinery-leak | S | official | The figures below for {settlement} were taken some time ago and may not show what has happened since. | |
| DS-ECO-10 / FRESHNESS CAVEAT / v2 | These counts stand as the town's own last reckoning of itself, and a later change may not be in them yet. | machinery-leak | S | official | These counts are {settlement}'s last reckoning, and a later change may not show in them yet. | |
| DS-ECO-10 / FRESHNESS CAVEAT / v3 | What is written here was true at the survey. Nobody has recounted it. | machinery-leak | F | official | What is written here was true when it was written. Nobody has counted again since. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Plains / v1 | {settlement} sits on ground that grows things without argument, and the town's whole arrangement follows from that one fact. | aphorism | S | local | {settlement} sits on open ground that grows things easily, and most of the town lives by farming it. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Plains / v3 | There is nothing between this town and the horizon but what the town has planted, and the town knows both halves of that. | mannered | S | local | Around {settlement} there is nothing but the town's own fields to the horizon, and nothing to hide behind. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Hills / v3 | The work here is uphill in the literal sense, and the town prices its carting accordingly. | mannered | S | trade | Everything at {settlement} is carted uphill, and carting costs more here for it. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Mountains / v2 | The town sits in a place that had to be argued for: high, cold, and holding something worth the height. | mannered | S | local | {settlement} sits high and cold, and it is there for what the rock holds. | needs: the ore or stone named |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN River / v1 | The water is {settlement}'s road and its mill both, and the town's earnings divide along it rather than around it. | mannered | S | local | The river is {settlement}'s road and its mill-race, and most of the town's money is made along it. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Desert / v1 | {settlement} exists where it does because of water rather than soil; the ground gives almost nothing and the town's whole economy is arrangement. | mannered | S | local | {settlement} is where it is because of water. The ground gives almost nothing; everything else is carried in or carefully made. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Desert / v2 | What surprises a stranger at {settlement} is not the dryness but how thoroughly the town has organised itself around it. | no-evidence | S | local | Everything at {settlement} is arranged around water: who draws it, when, and how much. | needs: the town's water source |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Swamp / v1 | The ground under {settlement} will not carry a heavy building or a straight road, and every trade the town has is shaped by that refusal. | mannered | S | trade | The ground under {settlement} will not carry a heavy building or a straight road, and every trade in the town works around that. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Swamp / v2 | A stranger reaches {settlement} by the one path that holds, and understands immediately why the town is where it is and not a mile in any direction. | mannered | S | local | Only one path into {settlement} holds firm, and the town stands exactly where that path ends. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Swamp / v3 | What this country gives, it gives to people who know it, and the knowing is most of what the town sells. | mannered | S | local | The marsh gives a living only to those who know it, and the people of {settlement} sell that knowledge as guides and gatherers. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Tundra / v1 | The working year at {settlement} is short and the storing year is long, and the town's accounts are really an argument about that ratio. | mannered | S | local | At {settlement} the working season is short and the storing season long, and the whole year is planned around what must last the winter. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN default / v2 | Nothing about {settlement}'s ground explains {settlement}; the town is the explanation. | aphorism | S | local | There is nothing special about the ground around {settlement}; the town has made its living by work, not by luck of place. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / STRENGTHS none / v3 | {settlement} has no advantage of any kind and is still here, which is a plainer achievement than a good harvest. | aphorism | S | local | {settlement} has no advantage of any kind and has lasted anyway, on work alone. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / STRATEGIC VALUE / v1 | What Portaminus is worth to anybody else is a separate question from what it is worth to itself, and the two answers are not close. | aphorism | F | newcomer/outsider | Others want {settlement} for what it controls. The people who live there see little of that value. | needs: strategic-value facts as slots |
| DS-ECO-11 / STRATEGIC VALUE / v2 | A stranger with an eye for such things works out what this hamlet is worth holding well before anybody explains it. | no-evidence | S | traveller | Anyone who looks at {settlement}'s position can see why someone would want to hold it. | needs: what it controls |
| DS-ECO-11 / STRATEGIC VALUE / v3 | The village knows what it sits on, and knows that knowing is not the same as being able to keep it. | aphorism | S | local | The people of {settlement} know what their ground is worth to others, and know they could not keep it against anyone who came for it. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / UNEXPLOITED high / v2 | A stranger who knows what {resource} fetches elsewhere will find the sight of it lying unworked here difficult to leave alone. | mannered | S | trade | {resource} lies unworked around {settlement}, and anyone who knows its price elsewhere will want to change that. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / UNEXPLOITED high / v4 | The {resource} sits at the edge of being worth a great deal to {settlement}: what the town lacks is not the ground, it is the {institution} that would begin the work. | mannered | S | trade | {settlement} has the {resource}; what it lacks is {institution} to start the work. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / UNEXPLOITED medium-low / v1 | The city leaves stone unworked, and the leaving is a reasonable decision. What it would fetch would barely cover the working. | mannered | S | trade | {settlement} leaves its {resource} unworked. It would fetch barely enough to pay for the digging. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / UNEXPLOITED medium-low / v3 | The stone has been available to Copanucan for as long as the village has been here and has never been taken up; the arithmetic has been done and done again. | mannered | S | trade | {resource} has been there for {settlement} to take for as long as the town has stood, and nobody has found it worth the trouble. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / PARTIALLY EXPLOITED / v2 | A stranger tracing {resource} out of {settlement} finds it leaving half-finished, and finds nobody in the town troubled by that. | mannered | S | trade | {resource} leaves {settlement} half-finished, and nobody in the town is troubled by it. | |
| DS-ECO-11 / FULLY EXPLOITED / v2 | Nothing about this line is left on the table at Al-karez. A stranger following it from ground to gate finds every stage of it occupied. | mannered | S | trade | Every stage of the {resource} trade at {settlement} is done here, from the ground to the gate. | |
| DS-ECO-12 / ONE SOURCE / v3 | Copanucan rests on one earner. Nothing about that is failing; everything about the village's next hard season depends on it not failing. | aphorism | S | trade | {settlement} lives on one trade. If that trade has a bad year, the whole town has a bad year. | needs: the leading source's name as a slot. CLAIMS regex `Nothing about that is failing` must move with it |
| DS-ECO-12 / BROAD SPREAD / v2 | A stranger cannot say what {settlement} is for, and the reason is that the town is not for any one thing. | mannered | S | trade | {settlement} is not known for any one trade; it lives on many small ones. | |
| DS-ECO-12 / BROAD SPREAD / v3 | {settlement} would barely notice losing any single trade and could not replace several, which is a different exposure from the obvious one. | aphorism | S | trade | {settlement} would hardly notice losing one trade, but could not stand losing several at once. | |
| DS-ECO-12 / CRIMINAL PRESENT / v1 | A real portion of what Portaminus earns is earned by people who would not describe the earning to a clerk, and the books show the difference as a gap rather than a line. | mannered | S | local | A real portion of {settlement}'s money is made by people who keep it from the clerks, and it never shows in the town's books. | |
| DS-ECO-12 / CRIMINAL LEADS / v2 | What keeps this town solvent is the part of it nobody will name, and the naming is what would cost somebody. | aphorism | S | trade | What keeps {settlement} solvent is a trade nobody names aloud, because naming it would get someone hurt. | |
| DS-ECO-12 / CRIMINAL LEADS / v3 | A stranger doing honest business at Shangjin is competing with the town's principal industry and has not been told which one that is. | mannered | S | trade | Honest traders in {settlement} are up against the town's biggest business, and nobody has told them what it is. | {faction} is declared and could name it (DM-only) |
| DS-ECO-12 / LOCAL PRODUCTION / v3 | The town makes its own ordinary things and buys its extraordinary ones, which is the arrangement of most towns and is worth stating plainly. | mannered | S | trade | {settlement} makes its own everyday goods and buys in the rest. | |
| DS-ECO-12 / ENTREPOT / v1 | Some of what leaves {settlement} as an export never belonged to the town. It arrived, was counted, and went on, and the earnings are in the counting. | mannered | S | trade | Some of what leaves {settlement} as export was never the town's: it came in, was counted and went on, and the town is paid for handling it. | |
| DS-SUP-3 / COMPLETE / v2 | A stranger arriving at {settlement} with an ordinary need (a bed, a meal, a mended strap) finds all three without asking twice. | mannered | S | traveller | Anyone arriving at {settlement} with an ordinary need (a bed, a meal, a mended strap) finds all three without asking twice. | |
| DS-SUP-3 / ONE MISSING / v2 | A stranger will find everything he expects at {settlement} except one, and will be told, cheerfully, where the nearest one is. | mannered | S | traveller | Everything a traveller expects at {settlement} is here but one, and people will cheerfully tell you where the nearest is. | needs: the missing category's name |
| DS-SUP-3 / ONE MISSING / v3 | {settlement} is one house short of being a complete town of its rung, and the missing one is the sort a single arrival would supply. | machinery-leak | S | official | {settlement} lacks one house a place its size usually has, and one newcomer could fill the gap. | needs: the missing category |
| DS-SUP-3 / SEVERAL MISSING / v1 | {settlement} falls short of its own rung in more than one direction; what the town offers is a smaller list than a place this size is assumed to keep. | machinery-leak | S | official | {settlement} offers fewer services than a place its size usually does. | needs: the absent categories |
| DS-SUP-3 / SEVERAL MISSING / v3 | A stranger provisions carefully before leaving {settlement}, because a town this size is normally the last place it is necessary to. | mannered | S | traveller | Travellers stock up before they reach {settlement}; a place this size cannot supply all they would expect. | |
| DS-SUP-3 / HEALING GAP / v3 | The town manages ordinary hurts and has nothing at all for the extraordinary one, and the line between the two is where the arrangement fails. | aphorism | S | local | {settlement} can set a bone or bind a cut, but has nothing for a serious wound or illness. | |
| DS-SUP-3 / FOOD GAP / v2 | A stranger at {settlement} eats what he brought or what somebody offers him, and the offering is a favour rather than a trade. | mannered | S | traveller | Visitors to {settlement} eat what they brought or what someone offers; there is nowhere to buy a meal. | |
| DS-SUP-3 / CATEGORY IMPAIRED / v1 | Market square at Shangjin is open and short of what it works with; the service stands and what it can actually do has narrowed. | mechanism-first | S | trade | {institution} at {settlement} is open but short of supplies, and it can do less than it used to. | {institution} fills with a catalogue name ("Market square") |
| DS-SUP-3 / CATEGORY IMPAIRED / v3 | What {institution} can offer {settlement} shrinks with each turn the supply behind it stays short, and nothing in hand is turning that round. | machinery-leak | S | trade | {institution} can offer {settlement} less each month its supplies stay short, and nobody is putting it right. | "turn" plus the "nothing in hand" tic |
| DS-SUP-3 / METROPOLIS COMPLETE / v1 | {settlement} keeps every category a settlement can keep (including the ones a town has no business having), and the completeness is itself a fact about the place. | aphorism | S | local | {settlement} has every kind of service there is, including some no ordinary town would keep. | |
| src/generators/economy/foodBalance.js :: stressNotes (mass_migration) | Mass migration: an influx of newcomers stresses the food balance beyond normal needs. | mechanism-first, duplicate-surface | F | local | Newcomers keep arriving in {settlement}, and every one of them has to be fed from the same fields. | needs: settlement name in scope. 15 prints in 3 of 12 (×5 per dossier) |
| src/generators/economy/foodBalance.js :: stressNotes (famine, named source) | Famine: the fishing failed, taking 65% of local food production. | mechanism-first | F | local | The fishing failed this year, and with it about two thirds of the food the town catches and grows. | Band the figure. Also appended to the economic-backbone lead (×5) |
| src/generators/economy/foodBalance.js :: stressNotes (famine fallback) | Famine: crop failure has reduced local food production to 35% of normal capacity. | mechanism-first | F | local | The crops failed. The town will harvest about a third of what it needs, and the winter will be hungry. | |
| src/generators/economy/foodBalance.js :: stressNotes (under_siege) | Siege: external supply lines severed and outlying farmland abandoned or razed. | mechanism-first | F | watch | The siege has cut every road in, and the outlying farms are abandoned or burned. | |
| src/generators/economy/foodBalance.js :: stressNotes (plague_onset) | Plague: agricultural workforce decimated by illness, fields understaffed. | mechanism-first | F | local | The plague has killed or sickened many of the people who work the fields. The harvest will come in short, and the winter will be hard. | the owner's own example |
| src/generators/economy/foodBalance.js :: stressNotes (occupied) | Occupation: occupying forces consume approximately 20% of food supply beyond normal needs. | mechanism-first | F | local | The occupying soldiers eat from the town's stores, about a fifth more mouths than the town would otherwise feed. | |
| src/generators/economy/foodBalance.js :: stressNotes (wartime) | Wartime: conscription has thinned the agricultural workforce, cutting local output. | mechanism-first | F | local | The levy has taken men from the fields, and the town will harvest less this year. | |
| src/generators/economy/foodBalance.js :: stressNotes (slave_revolt) | Slave revolt: labour-dependent production is disrupted and outlying fields lie idle. | mechanism-first | F | local | With the enslaved workers in revolt, the outlying fields lie idle and nobody is harvesting them. | |

##### Models (lines that already carry the law — up to 8, with one clause why)
- DS-ECO-9 / ACTIVE FAMINE / v3: "A stranger is not fed here now. Whatever {settlement} has left is not being offered to anyone who does not belong to it." It shows the famine by what a visitor runs into.
- DS-ECO-2 / GRANARY nearly empty / v1: "The granary is very nearly out. What remains is counted daily and issued against a list." Plain fact first, then something you could actually see.
- DS-ECO-2 / FOOD deficit / v2: "Bread here is bought, not simply had. The fields do not cover the town and everyone knows which day the carts come." It carries the dependence through a street detail.
- DS-ECO-5 / ENTREPÔT / v2: "What passes through here does not stay long enough to be made into anything. The warehouses are full and the workshops are not." A visible contrast holds the whole fact.
- DS-ECO-1 / C1 / v3: "Nobody here talks about the price of bread. The town is well off, the {access} keeps it that way, and the talk is about what to build next." It shows wealth through what people talk about.
- DS-ECO-11 / TERRAIN Forest / v2: "The trees come nearly to the doorframes at {settlement}, and the town has arranged its light, its roads and its watch around them." Concrete, and it wears well as a frame.
- DS-ECO-10 / POSTURE established / v2 (lit): "The yards here are full of goods bound out and no two of them are the same. This is a town that sells." Evidence first, judgment after.
- DS-SUP-3 / FOOD GAP / v1: "There is nowhere in {settlement} that sells a meal, which is unusual at any size and worth a stranger knowing before dark." Practical stakes put plainly.

##### Notes (≤10 lines: patterns, a pool that needs variants, anything PLAUSIBLE vs CONFIRMED)
1. The worst pattern is "A stranger …" and the "stranger" lens: 30 of 346 variants open with "A stranger", and 42 use the word. Next are price and accounts words: "reckoning" ×9, "arithmetic" ×5, "the books / the column / the counting". The "X rather than Y" balance appears 30 times.
2. The code-side food and crisis writers are all mechanism-first "Label: system noun" sentences (foodBalance stressNotes). They then print about 5× per dossier, once the history lead and the viability verdict carry them along (CONFIRMED in sample 0).
3. CONFIRMED coupling: crisisBlindFindings.js :: CLAIMS writes no prose (not counted as a line). Its economy regexes quote annex rows verbatim: SECURE v1/v2/v3, IMPORT-DEP v3, POOR v2, C1 v3, MODERATE v2/v3, C3 v3, ECO-12 one-source v3, ECO-10 established v1 and COMFORTABLE v1. Any adopted rewrite of SECURE v3, one-source v3 or established v1 must move its CLAIMS row in the same change.
4. CONFIRMED: the income bar's percentage and its own desc figure disagree (14% vs 11%, sample 0). The shadow share is also told four times on one page: header, drag desc, income desc and DS-ECO-6.
5. CONFIRMED: DS-ECO-11 strategic and exploitation lines print joined in one paragraph, so two tic sentences stack. The `{institution}` slot fills with catalogue names ("Mill finishes it", "Market square at Shangjin").
6. These pools need slots more than ornament (+variants): ECO-12 one-source (the source's name), ECO-11 strengths (the strengths), ECO-11 strategic value (what it controls), SUP-3 one/several missing (the category names).
7. PLAUSIBLE: `{access}` for `isolated`/`mountain_pass` may render as an enum-ish word in ECO-1 C2 and ECO-10 vulnerable v2. I did not see this in the sample.
8. Dead code: tabHelpers.js :: foodNarrative (port/river/isolated) has no caller, so it is not audited. Chrome labels "Agriculture modifier: 40%", "Stress penalty: ×0.35" and PDF "Ag mod 0.4" are machinery labels, not sentences, so they are not counted.
9. DS-ECO-6 v0, ECO-7 v0 and ECO-10 caveat are frozen or canonical copy (R-DST-C). Their rewrites are proposals for the owner's veto only.

| DS-POW-2 / critical / v04 | A stranger arriving at {settlement} is told plainly by the first person he asks that nobody is in charge in the way he means it. | mannered | SLOTTED | traveller | Ask the first person you meet at {settlement} who is in charge and they will tell you plainly: nobody, not really. | — |

| src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js :: buildInsurgencyNarrative (outmuscled) | The commons no longer accept the authority's account of events. Inflammatory pamphlets are being distributed. | src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js :: buildInsurgencyNarrative (institutional) | The challenge to the governing faction is institutional, not popular. The governing faction has noticed and is considering whether to act before the coalition is complete. | machinery-leak, mechanism-first | FRAME | official | The trouble is inside the hall, not in the streets. Officials drag their feet on orders, taxes are collected but not handed over, and certain people are meeting in private. The hall has noticed and must decide whether to act before they are ready. | the instigator's house (Crisis tab has it) |
| src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js :: buildWartimeNarrative (supplied) | The war is present here as money and absence. The garrison has doubled in size and is well-supplied. The crown is paying for this one. Contracts for grain, leather, and ironwork are flowing to anyone with the capacity to fill them. The men who left to fight have not returned, which is a grief that runs beneath the commerce. Grand Merchant Oligarchy is navigating the difference between what it can extract for the war effort and what the settlement can actually spare. | mannered, mechanism-first | FRAME | trade | The garrison has doubled and the crown pays its bills, so contracts for grain, leather and ironwork go to anyone who can fill them. The men who left to fight have not come back. {governing} must decide how much more the town can give. | — |
| src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js :: buildWartimeNarrative (short) | The war is present here as scarcity and fear. Conscription has taken workers, not soldiers. The farms and workshops feel their absence. Supply caravans pass through on crown requisition and local needs come second. … The governing faction signed the order. There was no alternative that anyone could see. | mannered, machinery-leak | FRAME | trade | Conscription has taken the workers, and the farms and workshops are short-handed. Crown supply carts take what they need first; prices have risen and will rise again. A crown officer left last week with a list for next month, and the hall signed it. | — |
| src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js :: buildReligiousConversionNarrative (v1) | The schism is now formal. | src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js :: buildReligiousConversionNarrative (v2) | The conversion order came from {governing} and was formally acknowledged within the week. The depth of the actual compliance is a different question. … | aphorism | FRAME | official | The order to convert came from {governing} and was obeyed on paper within the week. The old faith no longer holds services openly; nobody believes it has stopped holding them. | — |
| src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js :: buildMigrationNarrative (strong economy) | The settlement is receiving more people than its infrastructure was built for. | src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js :: buildMigrationNarrative (exodus) | Three families left this week. Two more last week. The departure is quiet and orderly, which makes it worse. | src/generators/power/governanceNarrative.js :: buildSlaveRevoltNarrative | The revolt began at the settlement's labour system and has not been contained. Its private position involves considerably more urgency. | machinery-leak, mechanism-first | FRAME | watch | The revolt began among the bonded labourers and has not been put down. The hall was slow to act because nobody wanted to admit how organised the rebels were. ##### Models (lines that already carry the law — up to 8, with one clause why)
- `governanceNarrative.js :: buildMigrationNarrative (influx)` — "More families arrived this week, and more the week before. The work they came for is not here, and every household taken in is one more the stores must cover. Elder Council is being asked to choose…": evidence, cost and the coming choice, in plain words.
- `governanceNarrative.js :: buildStressNarratives.famine (no-institution arm)` — "The last harvest failed badly. What remains is being rationed by whoever holds the stores. Neighbours who shared meals last winter are watching each other carefully.": the owner's own harvest shape.
- `governanceNarrative.js :: deriveBaselineConflict (royal levies)` — "The crown has called in military levies from the noble houses. Two houses have complied. One has not, and has not explained why.": an event a DM can retell in one breath.
- `factionDynamics.js :: mil↔crim (working arrangement)` — "Patrol routes avoid certain streets. Certain arrests never happen.": shows the deal through what a visitor would notice.
- `DS-POW-6 / capture ADVANCING / v03` — "Money is being spent at Becanchihua on people who cannot account for having it, and the accounting is not being asked for.": evidence before the judgment.
- `DS-POW-2 / critical / v03` — "{settlement} is being governed hand to mouth. Each decision buys the {seat} the time to make the next one…": plain, concrete, carries the stake.
- `DS-POW-6 / stolen goods / v03` — "Things taken at {settlement} reappear in the market a season later with nothing wrong about them…": a thing you could see on a stall.
- `DS-POW-1 / DEFENSE adverse / v03` — "{settlement} keeps one plain accusation against the {seat} (that it has left the town open) and brings it out again whenever anything else goes wrong.": the town's own voice, no ornament.

##### Notes (≤10 lines: patterns, a pool that needs variants, anything PLAUSIBLE vs CONFIRMED)
1. CONFIRMED: the TSV's sampleTowns undercounts. Rows whose template says "town" print with the tier word swapped ("thorp", "metropolis"), so the matcher misses them. Rows 1, 12, 22, 30, 40, 46, 63, 124, 125, 143, 170 and 171 printed in the sample but are marked 0 (row 170 is the "quieter arithmetic" lead in 4 of 12 towns). The lit counts above use the corrected match.
2. Pattern 1, the "A stranger …" opener: 25 of 262 annex variants (nearly every block's `visitor` angle; 28 counting "stranger" mid-sentence). Each one is rewritten here to put the evidence first. The `visitor` angle needs a different first move, not a different noun.
3. Pattern 2, the faction web (factionDynamics) and the stress narratives speak like an analyst ("functional partnership", "structural allies", "institutional, not popular", "the governing faction"). 26 of 27 web lines are mechanism-first. They print under labels that already say the relation type.
4. CONFIRMED, slot collision: `{seat}` and `{faction}` resolve to the same body. Examples: "Grand Merchant Senate outweighs everyone who wants the Grand Merchant Senate"; "take the Elder Council"; "What Elder Council holds", with no article where `{seat}` gets "The".
5. CONFIRMED, duplicate-surface (fractured): one fractured town prints the same claim up to 4 times. Screen: the banner plus a DS-POW-1 variant. PDF: the footer plus the faction crisisNote. The powerStrata twin says "real decisions" twice in one note. The screen and PDF banners also disagree ("made informally" vs "No faction holds clear authority").
6. CONFIRMED, duplicate-surface (rumour): the relationship rumour prints on screen Overview, twice in the PDF (Overview "Notable connection" + Relationships) and again in Foundry. Screen↔PDF parity for the stability/recent-event lines is by design and is not counted.
7. PLAUSIBLE content bug, not style: the theocracy lens printed for a Noble Governorship ("The Noble Governorship at Olympambelos governs from a creed", soak1k-10). Separately, soak1k-4 shows the stability "Strained (people arriving or leaving)" beside the monster_pressure vignette, because the two precedence ladders disagree.
8. Pools that need +variants rather than ornament: DS-POW-6 neutral baseline (2 of 4 are the same line with walls/roads swapped), and DS-POW-2 siege (v1/v5 and v4/v6 are walls/no-walls twins). The heading "The quieter arithmetic" brings the measured "arithmetic" tic into a section title.
9. Scope: faction descriptions audited = rulingStructure's two governing-body tables (44) + the 3 stressFactions postures that append to them. The merchant/noble/military/criminal desc ladders in rulingStructure.js (≈40 strings, e.g. "Genuinely three-way territory") were not audited and show the same mechanism-first register (PLAUSIBLE). npcStructure goals (76) were R11-reviewed and pass except one.
10. Baked nouns in PASS lines (prints wrong outside its tier, not a voice fault): "The village is divided…", "The village reeve disagrees", and the noble↔gov line's "Council decisions".

| DS-DEF-2 / B&M: settled, defenses beyond the need / v05 | A stranger finds {settlement}'s works manned in earnest; what was built against nothing in particular is being held against something now. | mannered | SLOTTED | watch | {settlement}'s walls are manned in earnest now: works built against nothing in particular are being held against something. |  |
| DS-DEF-2 / B&M: settled, nothing organized / v03 | A stranger walks out of {settlement} in any direction at any hour and meets nothing that would justify a watch. | mannered | SLOTTED | traveller | You can walk out of {settlement} in any direction at any hour and meet nothing that would call for a watch. |  |

| DS-WAR-5 / resistance simmering / v03 | What is organized at {settlement} is waiting rather than idle, and waiting is a decision that can be reversed in an afternoon. | aphorism | SLOTTED | local | The resistance at {settlement} is organized and waiting, and could rise in an afternoon. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / blockade stamped (recorded cause) / v01 | The transport into {settlement} is impaired and the impairment has a name on it: {counterpart}'s blockade, and nothing about the routes themselves. | machinery-leak, mechanism-first | SLOTTED | trade | Goods are not getting into {settlement}, and the cause is {counterpart}'s blockade, not the roads. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / blockade LIFTED (the impairment is cleared) / v01 | The blockade on {settlement} has ended and what it impaired is running again; the town's supply has resumed, and what it lost while it was closed has not. | machinery-leak | SLOTTED | trade | The blockade on {settlement} has ended and goods are moving again; what the town lost while it was closed has not come back. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / blockade LIFTED (the impairment is cleared) / v03 | The blockade on {settlement} is gone and the town's condition has not returned with it, which tells the town something about what the blockade actually cost. | mannered | SLOTTED | local | The blockade on {settlement} is gone but the town has not recovered, and only now can see what the blockade really cost. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / a stream: true treaty term (the installment sh… / v01 | {settlement} pays {counterpart} by the season under {term}, and the paying executes itself. Nobody has to decide to do it, and stopping would be a decision. | machinery-leak | SLOTTED | official | {settlement} pays {counterpart} every season under {term}; it goes out without anyone deciding to send it, and stopping would take a decision. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / a stream: true treaty term (the installment sh… / v02 | What the town owes goes out on a schedule, {timeband_span} of schedule remaining, and it is the bill of the war, still being paid. | machinery-leak | SLOTTED | official | {settlement} sends what it owes on schedule, with {timeband_span} still to go: the bill for the war, still being paid. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / a stream: true treaty term (the installment sh… / v03 | The obligation at {settlement} executes itself each season, and stopping it would take a decision nobody in the hall has been willing to be the one to make. | machinery-leak, mannered | SLOTTED | official | The payment leaves {settlement} each season on its own, and nobody in the hall wants to be the one who stops it. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / a HIGH-burden term / v01 | What {term} takes from {settlement} is a real share of what the town has, and the taking is the largest single fact in its accounts. | mechanism-first, machinery-leak | SLOTTED | official | What {term} takes from {settlement} is a real share of everything the town has; it is the heaviest charge the town carries. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / a HIGH-burden term / v02 | The burden {settlement} carries under {term} is heavy enough to be shaping what the town becomes rather than merely what it can afford. | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | official | {term} takes enough from {settlement} to shape what the town becomes, not just what it can afford. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / aftermath occupation_lifted / v01 | {counterpart}'s garrison has left {settlement}. What the occupation arranged remains arranged, and the town is now responsible for arrangements it did not choose. | mannered | SLOTTED | watch | {counterpart}'s garrison has left {settlement}, but the rules it set remain, and the town must now run things it never chose. | needs: what the occupier set up |
| DS-WAR-5 / aftermath war_exhaustion / v02 | The town has not recovered and has stopped talking about recovering, which is a stage rather than a mood. | aphorism | SLOTTED | local | {settlement} has not recovered and has stopped talking about recovering. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / aftermath war_drain / v02 | What {settlement} spends on a war that is over is spending it cannot spend on anything else, and the list of anything else is getting longer. | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | official | Money {settlement} spends on a finished war is money it cannot spend on anything else, and the list of what goes without grows. | needs: what goes without |
| DS-WAR-5 / aftermath war_spoils / v03 | What {settlement} took is being turned into something permanent by the people who hold it, and the town's shape after that will not be the shape it had. | mannered | SLOTTED | local | The people holding {settlement}'s spoils are making them permanent, and the town will be changed by it. | needs: what the spoils are |
| DS-WAR-5 / aftermath relief_burden / v01 | {settlement} is carrying people it did not have before; the relief the town gave is now a standing cost, and the cost has outlasted the crisis that produced it. | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | newcomer | {settlement} is still feeding and housing the people it took in, long after the crisis that brought them. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / aftermath relief_burden / v02 | What was generosity at {settlement} has become arithmetic, and the town has begun to say so out loud. | mannered | SLOTTED | local | What began as generosity at {settlement} is now a cost, and people have begun to say so out loud. |  |
| DS-WAR-5 / aftermath relief_burden / v03 | The people {settlement} took in are still here, and the town's generosity has become a standing item that somebody has to fund. | machinery-leak | SLOTTED | newcomer | The people {settlement} took in are still here, and someone has to keep paying to feed them. |  |
| ● src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, occupied) | Movement is restricted and monitored. {curfewAuthority} curfew and checkpoint protocols. Common crime is suppressed by authoritarian presence. Residents face little risk from thieves and considerably more from informers and occupation officials. Resistance activity operates underground. | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | watch | Soldiers hold the checkpoints and enforce the curfew. Thieves are scarce now; the danger is informers and occupation officials, and the resistance works out of sight. |  |
| src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, under_siege) | Siege conditions have transformed the settlement's social character. {milRef} with increasing severity as supplies run low. Rationing disputes, black market food trading, and desperation theft are rising. | mechanism-first, machinery-leak | SLOTTED | local | {milRef} more harshly as the food runs down. People quarrel over rations, food is sold under the counter, and the hungry have started to steal. |  |
| ● src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, famine) | Hunger has destabilised the normal social order. {foodRef}. Desperation theft is rampant and difficult to distinguish from survival. | src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, plague_onset) | Disease has reorganised daily life around containment and fear. {quarRef}, with mixed compliance. Frightened neighbours turn on the sick, and medicine and burials cost whatever the seller asks. |  |
| src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, insurgency) | The settlement is experiencing organised resistance. Patrol patterns have changed. | ● src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, slave_revolt) | Active armed conflict between revolt participants and security forces in contested districts. | ● src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, wartime) | War has reorganised daily life. Strangers are viewed with heightened suspicion. | ● src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, recently_betrayed) | The settlement is processing a betrayal. Strangers are viewed with heightened suspicion. | ● src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, monster_pressure) | Monster pressure from the surrounding region has changed how the settlement operates after dark. Outlying areas are avoided. Night movement is restricted. | mechanism-first, machinery-leak | SLOTTED | watch | Creatures come close after dark. | src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, indebted) | Debt service obligations shape every civic decision. | ● src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, mass_migration) | The settlement is absorbing more people than its infrastructure was built for. | src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, religious_conversion) | The religious shift has divided the settlement. Each faction suspects the other of reporting to the relevant authority. | src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, crimeIsGovt) | There is no meaningful distinction between criminal organizations and civil authority here. {crimeRef} provides order of a sort: its own. | src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc, Very Safe) | Effective {lawRef} and low criminal activity make this among the safest settlements in the region. | src/generators/safetyProfile.js :: generateSafetyProfile (safetyDesc wallNote, Very Safe) | Walls and controlled entry points reinforce the guard's ability to monitor movement. | mechanism-first, duplicate-surface | SLOTTED | traveller | Everyone comes in by the gates, where the guard sees them. |  |

Higher is better. | machinery-leak, duplicate-surface | FRAME | local | How ready the town is for each kind of trouble; a longer bar is better. |  |
##### Models (lines that already carry the law — up to 8, with one clause why)
- DS-WAR-2 / relational · strained / v03 — "Fewer go each time from {settlement}, and fewer of those who go come back to the same houses." — the cost given as the people who do not come home.
- DS-WAR-2 / territorial · strained / v01 — "…the townspeople chafe, stones are thrown, and the throwing has stopped being the work of children." — evidence first, and the danger shown by who is throwing.
- DS-WAR-1 / statusLabel: Occupied / v01 — "The hall still sits, the market still opens, and neither of them decides anything that {counterpart} has an opinion about." — what a visitor sees, then what it means.
- DS-WAR-5 / aftermath trade_realignment / v02 — "The stalls at {settlement} are quieter than the town remembers them being, and the carters who used to fill them have somewhere else to be now." — the quiet stalls carry the economics.
- DS-DEF-6 / Naval Defense: Under blockade / v02 — "The harbour at {settlement} is full of vessels that cannot leave and empty of the ones that should be coming in." — one picture holds the whole blockade.
- DS-DEF-10 / posture COMMAND SPLIT / v03 — "Orders reach the gate at {settlement} from two directions, and the people at the gate have quietly decided which to obey." — the split command told through the people at the gate.
- DS-DEF-2 / Economic Survival: WEAK / v02 (lit) — "The people who would have to hold Albamunicipium through something are already owed, and they have not forgotten it." — money told as people who are owed wages.
- src/domain/display/defenseDisplay.js :: foodEnduranceLine — "No granary: under siege the outlying fields are lost; with what is still harvested, household stores carry the thorp for about two months at full rations, about six months on half." — plain, concrete, the town's own numbers, easy for a DM to retell.
##### Notes (≤10 lines: patterns, a pool that needs variants, anything PLAUSIBLE vs CONFIRMED)
1. Worst pattern, the "A stranger …" opener: 61 of the 670 annex variants open with "A stranger"/"What a stranger" (17 of them print in the sample), mostly over a good piece of evidence that only needs the opener dropped. The other named tics ("arithmetic", "nothing in hand", "two different things", "knows perfectly well", "not merely a posture", "going on being a") appear in 14 rows, and several of those print (DS-DEF-1 WEAK v3, DS-DEF-8 v1/v2, DS-DEF-11 STRAINED v2). CONFIRMED.
2. Second worst, the same sentence on many surfaces (duplicate-surface). The mass-migration summary prints 7 times in one dossier (screen Military status, the PDF cover and three more PDF places, Foundry twice). Its phrase "more people than its infrastructure was built for" is also written separately in safetyProfile.js and governanceNarrative.js, and prints again on the same Defense tab. The viability summary prints 4 to 5 times (screen, PDF headline with the enum prefix "viable."/"notViable.", PDF verdict, Foundry twice). CONFIRMED in soak1k-0 and soak1k-1.
3. On one Defense tab there are three sentences about walls controlling entry: DS-DEF-5 walls v2 + safetyDesc wallNote + guardEffectivenessDesc wallNote. CRIM_STRUCTURE_DATA.organized prints next to DS-DEF-4 organized v1/v4 in 3 of 12 towns (CONFIRMED), and its diffuse note repeats DS-DEF-4 diffuse v1 (PLAUSIBLE: no co-print in this sample). The PDF Defense page prints "Plague spreads until it burns out" and then "Plague burns unchecked" a few lines apart. DS-DEF-2 is the screen restatement of threatAssessment.js's PDF rows, so screen and PDF say the same thing in two voices.
4. The sampleTowns column undercounts. Templates that say "The town" print the tier noun ("The thorp", "The city"), and some print with "It" in place of the name. 28 rows marked 0 actually print. For example DS-DEF-3 COMPOUND v1 prints in 7 of 12 towns and DS-DEF-5 charter ABSENT v2 in 2. Lit status here was measured by matching the templates against the samples. CONFIRMED.
5. Pools that need a slot or variants rather than ornament: DS-DEF-3 COMPOUND and DS-DEF-8 never name the crisis (needs: crisis noun). DS-DEF-10 "badge (any arm)" never names the arm ("this"). DS-WAR-2 "generic floor" never names the term (+variants). DS-DEF-1 strategic value HIGH never names the prize.
6. Grammar, outside the style brief but printed: DS-DEF-11 {defwork} gives "keeps a city walls and gates the present peace…" and "Al-karez's town walls stands…" (number agreement). PDF defenseHeadline prints "Defense for a thorpe:" (4 of 12 files). CONFIRMED.
7. Engine words in the voice of DS-DEF-7/8/10 and the viability prose: "posture", "arrangements", "the line", "the returns", "readiness", "entered rather than inferred", "historically plausible and institutionally functional", "first survey", "campaign advances". These sit inside sentences, not labels.
8. The code-side lines (safetyProfile safetyDesc, threatAssessment rows, viability summaries) are consistently mechanism-first: "X has reorganised daily life", "Economic base cannot support crisis response". The stressTypes.js viabilityNote family (23 entries) is written in the same telegraphic register. Three are audited here; the rest are PLAUSIBLY the same.
9. DS-WAR rows (dark) are on the whole better than DEF rows. Their tics are the "which is the difference between X and Y" closer and the "{counterpart} … arithmetic" tail.

(Reason counts are by primary code. Annex: 421 rows, 138 REWRITE. Rows shown under First-impression are not repeated in the Rewrite table. Three rows each cover two lines: the mass_migration summary and its twin, castSentence with bruntLeast, and the tension lead with the migration stress note. That is why 273 table rows hold 276 lines.)

##### Models (lines that already carry the law — up to 8, with one clause why)
- `PRESSURE_SENTENCES.mass_migration` weak arm [1]: "Every season brings more households to Albamunicipium and no more work. The newcomers camp where they can, and Elder Council is counting heads it has no plan for." — what happened, who, what it costs, in the town's own nouns.
- `STRESS_DESCS.mass_migration[4]`: "The road to Albamunicipium has acquired a verge of graves…" — evidence a visitor sees before any judgment.
- `PRESSURE_SENTENCES.famine[3]`: "The bakers of {settlement} have started cutting the bread with things that are not flour; everyone can taste it…" — one concrete thing carries the whole crisis.
- `RENDERERS.wartime` (loss): "Conscription has taken a third of the working-age men. Supply caravans pass through on crown requisition." — plain, countable, human.
- `DS-STR-2 / ORIGIN: leyline_silence / v03`: "Hedge wizards are leaving {settlement} quietly; the ones who stay have started learning herbcraft." — shows the cause through people.
- `DS-STR-2 / ORIGIN: council_schism / v02`: "Two officials claim the same seal, the same office and the same tax. The town pays whichever collector reaches it first." — the stakes as a scene.
- `DS-STR-1 / SLAVE REVOLT / v05`: "{settlement}'s rolls are wrong in a way the clerks cannot correct: the names are there and the people are not where the names say." — evidence first, then the meaning.
- `STRESS_DESCS.monster_pressure` (structure-free) "Fresh earth marks a row of graves at the edge of {settlement}, all dug in the same week…" — vivid only through the town's own facts.

##### Notes (≤10 lines: patterns, a pool that needs variants, anything PLAUSIBLE vs CONFIRMED)
1. DUPLICATE-SURFACE MAP, soak1k-0 (CONFIRMED by line grep): crisis summary "…receiving more people than its infrastructure…" ×7 (Defense military status, PDF cover, PDF Overview, PDF Tonight, PDF Defense, Foundry Overview, Foundry Crisis); historicalCharacter quote ×7 (Overview, History, PDF Overview, PDF History ×2, Foundry ×2); both tension lines ×6 each (Overview, History, PDF Overview, PDF Plot-hooks "Underlying tensions", PDF History, Foundry), and the stress note inside the first also on Viability (×7); cast hook "In the migration, the brunt falls on…" ×6 (Plot Hooks, Tonight, PDF Tonight, PDF Plot Hooks, Foundry Tonight + Plot Hooks); "Immigration:… Emigration:…" ×3 (Defense, PDF Tonight, Foundry); Tonight header, "Wants to get their own people…" and Settlement Origin ×3; crisis hook and "(1 crisis chip)" headline ×2. soak1k-3: famine summary ×7; "Short-term economic viability…" ×4.
2. CONFIRMED contradictions: famine summary says "second failed harvest season" where the record's failed source is the fishing and the granary holds five months (soak1k-3); "split." label printed over the "no direct stake" reason (crisisCast rule 11, `stake: 'split', reason: untouched`); mass_migration's "If nothing is done" prints the emigration half under an influx-only kind; the occupation "local face" clause ends "leads the occupying power".
3. PLAUSIBLE: a slave revolt prints on a hamlet ("A poor, critical — slave revolt hamlet", soak1k headline list) although `requiresTier: "town"`; ARITY v03 is tagged sampleTowns 0 but prints in soak1k-4; port.generic origin prints for a mountain thorp founded "at a strategic mountain pass".
4. ORDER: on the Overview the DS-CND trend line prints ABOVE the situation line, so every crisis Overview opens on an abstraction ("The trend is the wrong way…") before the people. Put the situation first and the trend after it.
5. The "If nothing is done" heading is fed by `viabilityNote`, which is economy telemetry, not a consequence. All 17 notes are rewritten as "If nothing is done, …" with the same facts; the heading wants its own field.
6. The crisis-cast reasons (bruntLeast, leverTemporary, leverInstitution, noLevers, nature) are the casting rule printed as prose; they want the BASE clause ("its people take in the newcomers"), which already exists in `CAST_REASONS.bruntBase`.
7. Pools needing variants rather than ornament: DS-CND-1 DIRECTION (3 lines print on every crisis Overview; 2 of the 3 worsening/stable lines are known tics); `stressTypes.mass_migration.crisisHook` (one aphorism among 14 concrete hooks); `POLITICAL_FLAVOR.stable` (aphorisms that print under the title in quotes on calm towns).
8. Tics in this section: "arithmetic" (6 lines), "two different things"/"a separate question" (3), "X is its own kind of Y"/"the indecision is a policy" (4), "the same document" (2, one per pool pair), "the condition" as an engine noun (9 DS-CND lines).
9. Not audited, flagged: `narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES` (History "Stress note", 45 lines) is almost all aphorism ("the arithmetic of names has broken", "this time the ground gets a vote"); `governanceNarrative.js :: buildMigrationNarrative` is a third copy of the migration summary.
10. The "The place" second-paragraph sentences are D2 rows (DS-GEN-12/17); only their 9 lit forms were read here, and only "job on the side" is rewritten. Faith lead and Origin mostly pass; Daily Life's "Ordinary life here in prose." is a button caption printing as the section lead.
