| **CODE ROWS** | | | | | | |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.under_siege[0] | What the founders built is now being tested by forces they could not have anticipated. The original reasons for settling here have become irrelevant to immediate survival. | mechanism-first, aphorism | FRAME | local | The founders chose this place for its trade, water and good ground. Now it is under siege, and none of that matters unless the walls hold. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.under_siege[2] | Every founding is a bet that a place can be kept. The wager has finally been called, generations after the founders stopped being able to lose it. | aphorism, mannered | FRAME | local | The founders built walls in case they were ever needed. They are needed now. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.famine[0] | The settlers who chose this land did so because it seemed fertile and promising. The current harvest failures would be unrecognisable to them. | mannered | FRAME | local | The settlers chose this land because it fed them well. Now the harvests are failing and families are going hungry. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.famine[1] | This ground fed the founders well enough to make them stay. The people living on it now measure it by a different arithmetic: not what it gave, but what it still owes. | mannered | FRAME | local | This ground fed the founders well enough to make them stay. This year it has not fed their grandchildren. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.famine[2] | The land that drew the first families here has not changed. The weather, the yields, and the margins have, and the founders left no instructions for this. *(lit)* | mannered, mechanism-first | FRAME | local | The land that drew the first families here is the same land, but the harvests have come in short, and the winter ahead will be hard. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.occupied[0] | The original settlement was founded with a degree of independence that no longer exists. The current administration answers to outside authority. *(lit)* | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | The founders governed themselves. Now foreign officers give the orders, and the town's own council answers to them. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.occupied[1] | The founders came here, in part, to be far from other people's authority. The distance is unchanged; the authority arrived anyway. | aphorism | FRAME | local | The founders came here to be far from other people's rule. Other people rule it now. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.occupied[2] | Whatever charter or courage the founding rested on, it assumed the settlement's decisions would be its own. That assumption has been formally corrected. | mannered | FRAME | local | The founders assumed the town would always make its own decisions. Under occupation, it no longer does. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.politically_fractured[1] | The founding required its people to pull in one direction. The pulling continues; the direction is now the dispute. *(lit)* | aphorism | FRAME | local | The founders pulled together in one direction. Their descendants work as hard, but no longer agree on where they are going. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.politically_fractured[2] | What the founders shared (a purpose, a danger, a road out of somewhere worse) held their descendants together only as long as it was remembered. It is no longer remembered the same way twice. | mannered | FRAME | local | A shared purpose held the founders together. Their descendants remember that purpose differently, and the town has split along those lines. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.indebted[0] | The original settlers built something valuable. Their descendants have borrowed against it until the debt outweighs the asset. | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | The first settlers built something worth having. Their descendants have borrowed against it until the town owes more than it owns. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.indebted[1] | The founders left an inheritance; each generation since has drawn on it a little more easily than the last. The ease was the warning nobody read. | aphorism | FRAME | local | Each generation since the founding has borrowed a little more against what the founders left. Now the lenders want it back. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.indebted[2] | What was built here was solid enough to borrow against, which is precisely what happened, repeatedly, until the building belonged to the borrowing. | mannered | FRAME | local | What the founders built was solid enough to borrow against, and it has been, again and again; the lenders now own much of it. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.recently_betrayed[0] | The founding required trust among a small group of people. That trust has recently been violated in a way that echoes the founding's original fragility. *(lit)* | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | The town was founded by a few families who trusted each other. One of their own has just broken that trust. | needs: who betrayed whom |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.recently_betrayed[1] | Settlements begin as promises kept between a few families. This one has just been reminded what it costs when the promise is broken from inside. | mannered | FRAME | local | This town began as a promise between a few families, and someone inside has just broken it. | needs: who betrayed whom |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.infiltrated[0] | The settlement was founded by people who knew each other. Somewhere in the current population, that familiarity is being exploited. | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | The founders all knew each other, and the town still trusts familiar faces. Someone living here now is using that trust to hide. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.infiltrated[1] | A town this size still runs on recognising faces. Someone has learned exactly how much that recognition can be made to carry. | mannered | FRAME | local | A town this size still trusts faces it knows, and one of those faces is not what it seems. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.infiltrated[2] | The founding families could account for every soul inside the walls. That accounting is still the settlement's habit, which is what makes the error in it so useful to whoever is the error. | mannered | FRAME | local | The founding families could name every soul inside the walls, and the town still assumes it can. That is how someone who does not belong goes unnoticed. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.plague_onset[0] | The settlers chose this location for its resources and access. Disease does not respect those original calculations. | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | The settlers chose this place for its roads and water. The sickness came in the same way. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.plague_onset[1] | The roads and river that made this a good place to settle are the same roads and river the sickness travelled. The founding advantage has changed sides. | aphorism | FRAME | local | The roads and river that made this a good place to settle are the ones the sickness travelled. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.plague_onset[2] | The site was picked so that things would arrive here easily. Things still do. | aphorism | FRAME | local | The site was picked so trade could reach it easily. The sickness reached it just as easily. | +variants (all three plague notes say the same thing) |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.succession_void[0] | The founding generation is gone. What remains is contested, in ways the founders did not anticipate and did not plan for. | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | The founding generation is gone, and nobody agrees who should lead now. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.succession_void[1] | The founders decided many things, but never who decides once they were gone; that gap has finally reached the head of the table. *(lit)* | mannered | FRAME | local | The founders settled many things but never who would rule after them. Now the seat is empty, and the families are fighting over it. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.succession_void[2] | Every institution here descends from someone who is dead, and the descent is precisely what is now in question. | aphorism | FRAME | local | Every office here was handed down from the founders, and now nobody can agree who it passes to. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.monster_pressure[0] | The founding required pushing into terrain that was not entirely safe. That calculation is being revisited. | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | The founders knew this ground was dangerous and settled anyway. Now the danger has come back to the fields. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.monster_pressure[1] | The first settlers weighed the danger of this ground against its worth and chose to stay. The weighing has reopened, and this time the ground gets a vote. | mannered | FRAME | local | The first settlers judged this land worth its dangers. With something coming out of the wild again, people are asking whether they were right. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.monster_pressure[2] | The land gave the founders room on terms nobody wrote down. Something out there has started enforcing them. *(lit)* | mannered | FRAME | local | The founders cleared this land from the wild. Now something from the wild is coming back for it. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.insurgency[1] | Whoever founded this place assumed that governing it would remain possible. Governing has quietly become the settlement's least settled question. | mannered | FRAME | local | The founders assumed the town could always be governed. Now armed townsfolk are refusing to be. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.insurgency[2] | The order the founders established outlived their reasons for it. What remains is the order alone, and orders alone do not hold. | aphorism | FRAME | local | The rules the founders set have outlived the reasons for them, and people have stopped obeying. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.mass_migration[0] | The founders built for a fixed number of people who knew each other. The population that now fills these walls is neither fixed nor familiar. *(lit)* | aphorism | FRAME | local | The founders built for a few families who knew each other. Now newcomers crowd the streets, and half the faces are strangers. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.mass_migration[1] | The settlement was measured, at its founding, for the families that made it. The measurement is still visible everywhere (in streets, wells, and patience), and everything about it is now too small. | mannered | FRAME | local | The town was laid out for the families that founded it. Its streets and wells were never meant for this many people. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.mass_migration[2] | The founders knew every name inside the walls. The walls remain; the arithmetic of names has broken. *(lit)* | mannered | FRAME | local | The founders knew every name inside the walls. Now no one does: too many have arrived too fast. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.wartime[0] | The settlement was founded for trade and quiet increase. It now serves a war effort that its founders never imagined and would not recognise. | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | The town was founded for trade and quiet growth. Now it feeds and arms a war. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.wartime[1] | Nothing in the founding of this place anticipated a war. Its site, its trades, its debts were all chosen for peace. The war did not consult the founders. *(lit)* | mannered | FRAME | local | Everything here was chosen for peace: the site, the trades, the debts. Now there is a war, and the town is paying for it. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.wartime[2] | The founders built a market town. Somewhere far away, a quartermaster reclassified it. | mannered | FRAME | local | The founders built a market town. Now the army takes what the market used to sell. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.religious_conversion[1] | The first stones were laid with prayers this settlement no longer says. What was promised in those prayers (land, duty, protection) has outlasted the saying of them. | mannered | FRAME | local | The first stones were laid with prayers the town no longer says, and the land and duties promised in them are now in dispute. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.religious_conversion[2] | The settlement was consecrated once, thoroughly, in a faith now out of favour. Consecration, it turns out, is easier to perform than to transfer. | aphorism | FRAME | local | The town was consecrated in a faith now out of favour, and nobody agrees what that old blessing still binds them to. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.slave_revolt[0] | The settlement was built on a labour it did not count as its people. That reckoning, deferred since the founding, has arrived all at once. | mannered | FRAME | local | The town was built by slaves it never counted as its own people. Now they have risen. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.slave_revolt[1] | From its first season this place ran on hands it never numbered among its own. The ledger was always going to be opened; the founding merely set the date. | mannered | FRAME | local | From its first season this place ran on enslaved hands. Those hands have turned against it. |  |
| src/data/narrativeData.js :: STRESS_NOTES.slave_revolt[2] | The founders built with labour they owned and prosperity they kept. Both clauses of that arrangement are now being renegotiated by the other party. *(lit)* | mannered | FRAME | local | The founders built with slaves and kept the profits. The slaves have risen, and they mean to take back both. |  |
| src/generators/historyGenerator.js :: buildHistoricalEvent ECHO_PREFIXES[0] 'Old records suggest ' | Old records suggest an innocent person has been identified as a spy and is about to be executed *(lit)* | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | At the time, an innocent man was named as a spy and condemned. | needs: past-tense hook variants; a prefix cannot fix a present-tense hook (an old record cannot show what is "about to" happen) |
| src/generators/historyGenerator.js :: buildHistoricalEvent ECHO_PREFIXES[1] 'A recently surfaced document implies ' | A recently surfaced document implies a series of 'accidents' targeting newcomers is being attributed to chance *(lit)* | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | A letter found this year shows that the 'accidents' that killed newcomers back then were no accidents. | needs: past-tense hook variants |
| src/generators/historyGenerator.js :: buildHistoricalEvent ECHO_PREFIXES[2] 'Family accounts passed down from the time claim ' | Family accounts passed down from the time claim a series of 'accidents' targeting newcomers is being attributed to chance *(lit)* | mechanism-first | FRAME | local | Families who lived through it still say the 'accidents' that befell newcomers then were deliberate. | needs: past-tense hook variants |
| src/generators/historyGenerator.js :: buildHistoricalEvent ECHO_PREFIXES[3] "An archivist's notes from that period reveal " | An archivist's notes from that period reveal a merchant is funding both the reform movement and the suppression of it *(lit)* | mechanism-first, mannered | FRAME | local | Records from that time show a merchant paid for both the reformers and the men sent to break them. | needs: past-tense hook variants |
| src/generators/historyGenerator.js :: buildHistoricalEvent ECHO_PREFIXES[4] 'Evidence that survived the years indicates ' | Evidence that survived the years indicates one of the loudest voices warning about infiltration is themselves an agent *(lit)* | mechanism-first, mannered | FRAME | local | It came out later that one of the loudest voices warning of spies had been a spy. | needs: past-tense hook variants |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.econ_crim_blur.desc[0] | {r} and {s} maintain a relationship that exists in legal grey territory. {r} provides legitimate cover, {s} ensures market access that official channels wouldn't permit. | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | trade | {r} gives {s} an honest front, and {s} gets {r}'s goods into markets the law would close to them. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.econ_crim_blur.desc[1] | The books of {r} are clean because {s} keeps certain entries off them; the arrangement benefits both parties and would survive scrutiny by neither. | aphorism | SLOTTED | local | {r}'s books are clean because {s} keeps certain entries off them, and both of them profit by it. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.econ_crim_blur.desc[2] | What {r} sells is respectability, and {s} is a steady customer. Goods move through channels no inspector has thought to draw on a map. | mannered | SLOTTED | trade | {s} pays {r} to look respectable, and their goods move by routes no inspector checks. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.econ_crim_exploitation.desc[2] | The "security fee" {s} collects from {r} buys exactly one thing: the absence of {s}. It is paid punctually. | mannered, aphorism | SLOTTED | local | {s} collects a "security fee" from {r}, and what it buys is that {s}'s people stay away. {r} pays on the day. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.mil_crim_corruption.desc[2] | {r}'s clearance record looks respectable because {s} decides which crimes get cleared. The price of the arrangement is itemised and paid on time. | mechanism-first, mannered | SLOTTED | local | {r}'s arrest record looks good because {s} chooses which crimes get solved, and {s}'s silver pays for the favour. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.econ_mil_contract.desc[0] | {s}'s company is retained by {r}'s interests on an exclusive basis. The arrangement works well enough that official channels have stopped asking questions. | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | trade | {s}'s company works for {r} and no one else, and has for so long that the magistrates no longer ask about it. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.rel_mil_crusader.desc[0] | Glykeria Kataphloron and Pulcheria Laskaris operate as a unified authority: temporal and spiritual power reinforcing each other. Dissent against one is dissent against both. *(lit)* | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | watch | {r} preaches and {s} enforces, and in this town crossing one means crossing both. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.rel_mil_crusader.desc[1] | Between {r}'s pulpit and {s}'s {body}, the settlement is governed twice over; the sermons and the patrol routes have a way of agreeing. | mannered | SLOTTED | watch | Between {r}'s pulpit and {s}'s {body}, the town is ruled twice over: what is preached on the holy day is enforced in the street that week. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.rel_mil_crusader.desc[2] | {r} blesses what {s} enforces, and {s} enforces what {r} blesses. Where one authority ends and the other begins is a question neither permits. | aphorism | SLOTTED | watch | {r} blesses what {s} enforces, and nobody in town dares ask where one's authority stops and the other's starts. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.rel_crim_fraud.desc[0] | {r}'s religious position provides {s}'s operation with legitimacy, meeting spaces, and an excuse for financial flows that would otherwise attract scrutiny. | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | trade | {r}'s shrine gives {s}'s crew a respectable face, a room to meet in, and an excuse for money nobody could otherwise explain. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.rel_crim_fraud.desc[1] | {s}'s money passes through {r}'s offertory and comes out consecrated; the meeting rooms are sanctified, the accounts are not examined. | mannered | SLOTTED | trade | {s}'s money goes into {r}'s offertory box and comes out clean, and nobody checks the shrine's accounts. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.rel_crim_fraud.desc[2] | {r} provides the sanctity, {s} provides the income, and the congregation provides the cover (unknowingly), which is what makes it work. | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | trade | {r} lends the holiness, {s} brings the money, and the congregation, knowing nothing, provides the cover. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.mag_crim_market.desc[0] | {r} provides {s}'s network with components, identification services, and the occasional forged document that only a skilled practitioner could produce. | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | trade | {r} supplies {s}'s network with reagents, false papers and the odd forgery only a trained practitioner could make. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.gov_econ_dependence.desc[0] | The settlement's civic functions run on credit extended by Chilaun Oirat. Oyuntuya Ghaznavid cannot take any action that seriously threatens Chilaun Oirat's interests, and Chilaun Oirat does not let this be forgotten. *(lit)* | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | trade | The town runs on money borrowed from {s}; {r} cannot cross {s}, and {s} makes sure {r} remembers it. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.gov_econ_dependence.desc[1] | {s} holds the settlement's debts and therefore, in every way that matters, its agenda; {r} governs within the margin {s} allows. *(lit)* | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | trade | {s} holds the town's debts and so sets its business; {r} governs only as far as {s} lets. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.gov_mil_friction.desc[0] | Albinus Plancus and Cara Moderatus disagree fundamentally about where civil authority ends and military authority begins. The argument has been running for years and occasionally generates paperwork. *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | watch | {r} and {s} have argued for years over where the council's say ends and the soldiers' begins, and neither has given ground. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.gov_mil_friction.desc[1] | {r} issues orders {s} regards as suggestions, and {s} takes actions {r} regards as insubordination; the settlement runs in the gap between. | mannered | SLOTTED | local | {r} gives orders {s} treats as suggestions, and {s} does things {r} calls insubordination. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.gov_mil_friction.desc[2] | The line between {r}'s authority and {s}'s has been redrawn four times and honoured never. Each keeps the map that favours them. | mannered | SLOTTED | local | The line between {r}'s authority and {s}'s has been redrawn four times, and each still follows whichever version suits them. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.peer_rivalry.desc[0] | {r} and {s} compete for the same resource, position, or reputation. The competition is vigorous but bounded. Yet. *(lit)* | machinery-leak, mannered | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} want the same thing and compete hard for it, but neither has let it turn into real hatred. | needs: the prize, as one noun |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.peer_rivalry.desc[1] | {r} and {s} want the same thing and cannot both have it. The contest is conducted with scrupulous courtesy and total commitment. *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} want the same thing and only one can have it; they stay polite and give no ground. | needs: the prize, as one noun |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.peer_rivalry.desc[2] | Every advance {r} makes is answered by {s} within the season; the rivalry has become its own institution, with rules neither wrote and both obey. *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | Every gain {r} makes, {s} answers within the season, and the town has learned to expect it. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.mentor_legacy.desc[0] | {r} shaped {s}'s career in ways that {s} has not fully acknowledged. The debt is real; the gratitude is complicated. *(lit)* | aphorism | SLOTTED | local | {r} made {s}'s career, and {s} rarely says so. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.mentor_legacy.desc[1] | {s} stands where {r} put them, by a ladder {r} built and {s} has since pulled up. Both remember it differently. *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | {s} owes their position to {r} and has since shut the door {r} opened; the two remember it differently. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.mentor_legacy.desc[2] | What {s} knows, {r} taught (including, inconveniently, how to outmanoeuvre a teacher). *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | {r} taught {s} everything, including how to get the better of {r}. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.mutual_leverage.desc[1] | What {r} knows would ruin {s}; what {s} holds would finish {r}. On this foundation they have built an excellent working relationship. *(lit)* | aphorism | SLOTTED | local | {r} knows enough to ruin {s}, and {s} holds enough to finish {r}, so they work together very carefully. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.mutual_leverage.desc[2] | {r} and {s} deal with each other like men carrying lit lanterns in a powder store, carefully, and with great mutual politeness. *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} deal with each other slowly and politely, each knowing one slip could ruin both. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.wary_alliance.desc[0] | {r} and {s} work together because the alternative (working against each other) is worse for both. The alliance holds as long as the calculation holds. *(lit)* | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} work together because fighting each other would cost them both more, and both know it. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.wary_alliance.desc[1] | Angelos Hyaleas and Theodoros Kurtikios are allies by arithmetic: each has counted the cost of the other as an enemy and found it too high. The friendship is warm, public, and entirely conditional. *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} are allies because each has reckoned what the other would cost as an enemy. The friendship is warm, public and conditional. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.wary_alliance.desc[2] | The alliance between {r} and {s} was born of a shared threat and has outlived it; both are quietly recalculating what it is now for. *(lit)* | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} joined against a threat that is now gone, and each is wondering what the alliance is still for. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.genuine_respect.desc[0] | Panteleimonissa Bryennios and Thodoros Makrembolites have earned each other's regard through demonstrated competence. They don't agree on everything but they trust each other's judgment within their respective domains. *(lit)* | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} have each watched the other do good work. They argue, but each trusts the other's judgment in their own trade. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.old_debt.desc[0] | {r} owes {s} something significant: a favour, a secret kept, a crisis managed. The debt has never been formally acknowledged. It doesn't need to be. *(lit)* | machinery-leak, aphorism | SLOTTED | local | {r} owes {s} a great deal, and neither has ever said so aloud. | needs: what the debt is, as one phrase |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.old_debt.desc[1] | Years ago Angelos Hyaleas did for Ioanna Kantakouzenos the kind of favour that cannot be repaid, only serviced. Ioanna Kantakouzenos has been servicing it ever since. *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | Years ago {r} did {s} a favour too large to repay, and {s} has been paying it back in small ways ever since. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.old_debt.desc[2] | Between Stephanos Bryennios and Georgios II Phokas stands an old kindness with the structure of a mortgage. Neither names it; both know the balance to the penny. *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | {r} owes {s} for an old kindness. Neither mentions it, and both know exactly how much is still owed. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.family_complication.desc[0] | Chrysostomissa Kalojan and Glykeria Kataphloron are connected by blood or marriage in a way that neither finds professionally convenient. The connection is known; its implications are not discussed. *(lit)* | machinery-leak, mechanism-first | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} are family, which suits neither of their careers; everyone knows it and nobody mentions it. | needs: blood or marriage, which |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.family_complication.desc[2] | {r} and {s} share a family and avoid sharing anything else; every professional dealing between them is conducted as though the relation were a rumour. *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} are family, but at work they deal with each other as if they were strangers. |  |
| src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.bitter_history.desc[1] | {r} and {s} were something to each other once (partners, friends, perhaps more), and the wreck of it is the one subject on which both are permanently silent. *(lit)* | machinery-leak | SLOTTED | local | {r} and {s} were close once, and whatever broke between them is the one subject neither will discuss. | needs: what they were to each other |
| src/data/narrativeText.js :: POLITICAL_FLAVOR.stable ("no monuments") | Shaped by events that left no monuments but changed things nonetheless: the kind of history that only becomes visible in the way people behave *(lit)* | aphorism, no-evidence | FRAME | local | No great event marks this place; its history shows in people's habits, not in monuments. | +variants |
| src/data/narrativeText.js :: POLITICAL_FLAVOR.stable ("less ordinary ones") | An ordinary settlement that has outlasted several less ordinary ones nearby: through no single great decision, but through the accumulated effect of many small adequate ones *(lit)* | aphorism | FRAME | local | An ordinary town that has outlasted grander neighbours by getting the small things right, year after year. | needs: a neighbour's name |
| src/data/narrativeText.js :: POLITICAL_FLAVOR.catastrophic ("founding myth, except") | The people here carry the Occupation the way other places carry a founding myth, except this one is remembered accurately, by people who were there, which makes it a heavier thing to live beside *(lit)* | mannered | SLOTTED | local | The Occupation is this town's defining story, and the people who lived through it are still here to tell it. |  |
| src/data/narrativeText.js :: POLITICAL_FLAVOR.stable ("records are well-kept") | The kind of place where records are well-kept and disputes are resolved through precedent, not because the people are unusually reasonable, but because they have learned the cost of the alternative *(lit)* | aphorism | FRAME | local | Records here are kept carefully and disputes settled by precedent; the town learned the hard way what happens otherwise. |  |
| src/data/narrativeText.js :: POLITICAL_FLAVOR.catastrophic ("did not destroy the settlement") | The Uprising did not destroy the settlement. It destroyed the settlement's sense of itself. What came after was rebuilt from the ground up, including what people believed about where they lived *(lit)* | mannered, aphorism | SLOTTED | local | The Uprising left the buildings standing and changed everything else; the town was rebuilt afterwards, down to what people believe about it. |  |
| src/data/narrativeText.js :: POLITICAL_FLAVOR.catastrophic ("founding trauma") | The Great Fire is the settlement's founding trauma, not the actual founding, which is older, but the event that made everything before it feel like a different place *(lit)* | mannered, duplicate-surface | SLOTTED | local | The town is older than the Great Fire, but people here date everything from it: before the Fire, and after. |  |
| src/data/narrativeText.js :: POLITICAL_FLAVOR.stable ("historians overlook") | The kind of place that historians overlook and travellers remember fondly: no dramatic history, but a persistent one *(lit)* | aphorism | FRAME | local | No dramatic history here, only a long steady one, and travellers remember the place kindly. | +variants |
| src/data/narrativeText.js :: POLITICAL_FLAVOR.religious_heavy ("never fully healed") | The Religious Conflict divided this settlement's relationship to faith in ways that never fully healed: the institution persisted; the unity didn't *(lit)* | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | local | The Religious Conflict split the town's faith; the temple survived, but its congregation never came back together. |  |
| src/data/narrativeText.js :: POLITICAL_FLAVOR (succession: "changed hands at least once") | A settlement where power has changed hands at least once within living memory. The Succession Crisis left a governing structure that still hasn't fully settled *(lit)* | mechanism-first | SLOTTED | local | Power here changed hands within living memory, and since the Succession Crisis nobody has been sure who really rules. |  |

| src/generators/narrativeGenerator.js :: settlement-reason tier line (metropolis) | At this scale, the settlement no longer serves a single economic function. It IS the economic function for its region. *(lit)* | aphorism, duplicate-surface | FRAME | trade | Everything in the region is bought, sold or decided here. |  |
| src/generators/narrativeGenerator.js :: settlement-reason tier line (city, no deficit) | Large enough to produce what it consumes and consume what it produces. | src/data/cultureProfiles.js :: <culture>.scope ("… design grammar.") | A masonry, civic-square, patronage-and-law design grammar. *(lit)* | machinery-leak | FRAME | traveller | Stone towns built round civic squares, run by patrons and lawyers. | (per-culture plain phrase; drop the words "design grammar") |
##### Models (lines that already carry the law — up to 8, with one clause why)
- DS-GEN-3 / foodSecurity Deficit / v03: "The granary at {settlement} is open and the queue at it is longer than the stock behind it." (the evidence is the verdict)
- DS-GEN-3 / scores.military CRITICAL / v02: "{settlement} keeps no muster worth the name; the gate is shut at night by whoever is nearest to it." (one seen detail carries the whole score)
- DS-GEN-9 / event type: disaster / v01: "{settlement} lost {band} to {event} {timeband_since}, and the town has never rebuilt the quarter it happened in." (what happened, what it cost, what is still visible)
- DS-GEN-9 / event type: magical / v03: "{event} happened at {settlement} {timeband_since} and is now a story told to children with the frightening part left in." (plain, retellable, the town's own noun)
- DS-GEN-3 / prosperity Wealthy / v01: "{settlement} is rich in the way that shows in the paving and the doorframes before it shows in anybody's clothes." (wealth shown, not asserted)
- DS-GEN-1 / crime_wave / v04: "Whatever holds {settlement}'s streets after dark, it is not the watch; shopkeepers have begun buying their quiet directly, and the price of it is going up." (who pays, and what is coming)
- DS-GEN-3 / foodSecurity Surplus / v03: "What leaves {settlement} in carts is mostly food, and the carts leave full." (a visitor's view of a ledger fact)
- src/data/npcData.js :: STRESS_ECONOMIC_EFFECTS.mutual_leverage.desc[3]: "{r} and {s} trade favours on a strict count. Neither has ever been more than one favour ahead." (the concrete later variants of every archetype read like this; the first three of each do not)
##### Notes (≤10 lines: patterns, a pool that needs variants, anything PLAUSIBLE vs CONFIRMED)
1. CONFIRMED: the TSV's sampleTowns undercounts. The composer prints only the first clause of many lines (it cuts at ";", ", and", ":") and swaps a repeated {settlement} for "The {tier}". So 549/550/552/553/562/564/565/568/573/579/623–627/632 print in 1–3 towns while the TSV says 0. Rewrites for these rows are written so the first clause stands alone.
2. CONFIRMED worst surface: DS-GEN-14 prints in 12 of 12 towns, as the second line of History › Founding. Only three variants carry it (583 in 5 towns, 582 in 4, 579 in 3), and all three are aphorisms ("the editing is most of what a stranger sees", "the argument and all its revisions"). It sits under a STRESS_NOTES line in the same mannered register, so the founding block reads as two ornaments in a row. The pool needs +variants that carry the founding reason as a slot (`needs: the founding reason`).
3. STRESS_NOTES gives each crisis kind three variants that say one idea in three ornaments ("arithmetic of names", "both clauses… renegotiated", "the ground gets a vote"). The owner's plague model fits this slot exactly (founding, then what happened, then who it costs, then what is coming), and the FRAME rewrites above follow it.
4. Machinery words run through whole pools: "the record / rolls / readings / window / roll" (DS-POP-2, DS-POP-3, DS-GEN-11, DS-GEN-15/16), "first survey" (DS-GEN-11, plus FIRST_SURVEY_LABEL printed as the Viability lead), "within the mirror's reckoning" (610), "fates unresolved" and "relic ruin" (DS-GEN-8), and "design grammar" (cultureProfiles scope, printed in Identity).
5. Tic counts in these rows: "arithmetic" 7 annex + 3 code; "a stranger…" about 25; "which is its own kind of…" 4; "X and Y are two different things / both true" 5. "a stranger" appears in all three lit DS-GEN-11 and DS-GEN-14 lines.
6. ECHO_PREFIXES (CONFIRMED in 4 samples): the prefix puts a past source in front of a present-tense hook ("Old records suggest an innocent person… is about to be executed"). Rewording the prefix cannot fix the tense. The hook pools need past-tense variants, or the frame has to be dropped for hooks that read in the present.
7. Relationship desc pools: in all 18 archetypes, desc[0..2] are the abstract, mannered legacy lines, and they print often (old_debt "structure of a mortgage" in 8 towns, wary_alliance "recalculating" in 7). desc[3+] are concrete and pass. PLAUSIBLE fix: retire or rewrite desc[0..2] rather than add more.
8. Duplicate-surface. PLAUSIBLE: DS-GEN-6 tier overlay 313 and narrativeGenerator's metropolis line are the same claim, and the code line is the one that prints. Also PLAUSIBLE: 539 (Viability) and 233 (Systems Health) print the same "someone outside is paying" idea. CONFIRMED in the md export: Identity "Cultural notes" repeats the "Social texture" and "Built form" lines verbatim.

| JF-G-signed_to_lapse / * / v05 (unfolding) | There is nothing left to renew and nobody moving to renew it: the window opened, the chancery let the season pass, and the clerks entered that as a decision because there was nothing else to enter it as. | mannered | F | official | Nobody is moving to renew the agreement: the time to do it came and went, and the council let it pass. |  |
| JF-N-strain_to_coup / * / v01 (ledger) | {settlement}'s book carries two columns and joins neither: the instalments to {counterpart} left on time, every time, and the standing of the seat that sent them fell over the very same seasons. | machinery-leak | S | official | {settlement} paid {counterpart} every instalment on time, and over the same years its ruler lost the town's support. |  |
| JF-N-strain_to_coup / * / v02 (street) | {burden} stands on {settlement} and the standing of the seat that pays has fallen over the same seasons, and it is the hall that has joined the two: the book carries the columns and no line between them, and the book is what was read out at the verdict. | machinery-leak | S | official | {burden} weighs on {settlement} and its ruler has lost support for paying it; the hall blamed the ruler for the payments, and that was the case read out at the verdict. |  |
| JF-N-strain_to_coup / * / v03 (visitor) | A stranger at {settlement} will hear several accounts of what the town pays {counterpart} and no two alike; the terms are sealed, and the town has filled the gap with its own arithmetic. | mannered | S | traveller | Ask in {settlement} what the town pays {counterpart} and you hear several answers: the terms are sealed, and everyone guesses. |  |
| JF-N-strain_to_coup / * / v06 (counterforce) | Here the fall has a named cause and the clerks can point to the week: the seat lost standing for {reason}, exposed and entered, and the tribute and the war stood exactly where they had stood. | mannered | S | official | Here the ruler's fall has a plain cause the clerks can date: the seat lost support for {reason}, and the tribute and the war had nothing to do with it. |  |
| JF-N-burial_to_digup / * / v01 (ledger) | The rows against {counterpart} are in {settlement}'s book and are not counted when the hall counts {counterpart}: the war party can read them and cannot cite them, and the standing grievance reads {band} lighter for it. | machinery-leak | S | official | {settlement}'s old grievances against {counterpart} were buried by decree: the war party remembers them and is not allowed to cite them. | {band} dropped: engine quantity word |
| JF-N-burial_to_digup / * / v04 (elder) | {faction} has waited in the open since the decree was read, and the elders here know the difference between a grudge that is closed and one that is merely uncited. | mannered | S | official | {faction} has waited openly since the decree was read, and the elders know a buried grudge is not a closed one. |  |
| JF-N-burial_to_digup / * / v05 (unfolding) | A later hall at {counterpart} cited the rows anyway, word for word, and {timeband_since} the old {wound} is back in {settlement}'s book with a new one on top of it for the revoking. | machinery-leak | S | official | A later council at {counterpart} raised the old grievances anyway, and {timeband_since} the old {wound} is back between {counterpart} and {settlement}, with a new one for the broken decree. |  |
| JF-N-burial_to_digup / * / v06 (counterforce) | {counterpart} keeps its quarrel and {settlement} keeps the receipt: the price was paid in full against the wrong account (the carts went and were taken and thanked for), and the {temple} that witnessed the swearing has been asked about it twice since. | machinery-leak | S | trade | {counterpart} keeps its quarrel and {settlement} keeps the proof it paid: the carts went, were taken and thanked for, and {temple}, which witnessed the oath, has been asked about it twice since. |  |

##### Models (lines that already carry the law — up to 8, with one clause why)
- JF-CPL-1a / * / v01: "The market at {settlement} runs thin; the routes turned elsewhere when the {route} closed with the war, and they have not come back." — the owner's calibration: evidence, then cause, in plain words.
- JF-CPL-3a / * / v02: "The quarter nearest the {route} at {settlement} stands empty and is not being rebuilt; …" — opens on what a visitor can see.
- JF-CPL-2a / * / v01: "…the altar was raised before the walls were mended." — one concrete detail carries the whole judgment.
- JF-CPL-10b / * / v03: "The stalls at {settlement} are thin and the town is not hungry: …" — evidence first, then the plain reason.
- JF-SC-interdict / * / v02: "The bells at {settlement} keep the hours and nothing else. …" — a sound the players will hear, then the stakes.
- JF-SS-corner_and_mitre / * / v03: "The chapter house is the newest building in {settlement} and the one people here are least willing to discuss." — visible fact plus a social tell a DM can play.
- JF-P-petition_to_riot / * / v03: "The granary gates at {settlement} are new and the granary is not." — short, concrete, and still fresh after repeats.
- src/domain/dossier/hookLines.js :: SEAT_LINES[1]: "A vote on {issue} has been postponed three times, each time by whichever of {a} or {b} would have lost it." — a hook a DM can play tonight, with the town's own nouns in the slots.

##### Notes (≤10 lines: patterns, a pool that needs variants, anything PLAUSIBLE vs CONFIRMED)
1. Annex, CONFIRMED by count: the clerk/ledger conceit ("entered", "the record", "the book", "the clerks", "register", "roster") appears in 110 of 468 rows. Engine words (fold, join, mint, licence, bar, band, lane, rung/ladder, receipt, tier, "faith axis", "war book") appear in 55. Both cluster in the later families (JF-SS, JF-W, JF-T, JF-F, JF-I, JF-G, JF-N) and in the `ledger` and `counterforce` angles. The JF-CPL families mostly pass.
2. Annex: "A stranger …" / "Strangers at …" opens 30 rows, almost all in the `visitor` angle. That angle needs a different frame ("Visitors to {settlement} …", "Ask in {settlement} …", or start on the object itself) or it becomes a tic by the third town.
3. Hook repetition, measured: repetition-prose.json `hooks` gives all 0.182, sameTier 0.281, maxSameTier 0.55 (metropolis 0.529, city 0.319, thorp 0.313, town 0.31). Templates printed in ≥3 of the 12 sample PLOT_HOOKS sections: "A merchant they publicly opposed…" (8); "same group of faces" (6); "smuggling network is moving something unusual" (6); "A young trader has been undercutting them" (6); the crisis lever frame "the players can push on" (6 towns); "Three guards have gone missing" (5); "deposition in a civil case" (5); "They found something hidden" (5); "vigilantes have begun targeting innocents" (5); "The crime wave is coordinated" (5); "Something they were told was destroyed" (5); "Someone is buying up their debts" (5); "A single importer controls the grain…300%" (5); "A guard captain is on the payroll" (5); "An archivist's notes from that period reveal" frame (5); the brunt frame (4); "loses most if it gives" (4); plus 13 more at 4 and about 20 at 3 (the NPC_FACTION_LOYALTY and historyData pools carry no names, so every repeat reads verbatim).
4. The crisis hook frames are the worst hook rows. Their reason clauses are the engine's own vocabulary (CAST_REASONS / STAKE_REASONS), and the same sentence prints up to three times in one dossier: the Tonight hooks, the Crisis page "Who it touches", and the Plot Hooks chapter (duplicate-surface, CONFIRMED in soak1k-0). Lever names print the catalog label: "Inns and taverns (district)", "Blacksmiths (3-10)", "Military/Guard".
5. The PDF hooks lead tallies by `source` ("heaviest from tension"), but the chapter groups hooks as NPCs / History / Outlook, and the Foundry page groups them a third way (Tension / Economics / History / Npc). CONFIRMED in soak1k-0.
6. Thorp vs Thorpe, CONFIRMED: the label reads "Thorpe" (ruling F14), yet screen prose in the same dossier prints "thorp" 53 times, and the PDF prints "Thorpe-tier" and a lowercase "thorpe".
7. PLAUSIBLE: a "Disaster Event" heading sits over wartime hooks in soak1k-3 ("profits from wartime contracts"), which looks like an event-template/pool mismatch. Separately, soak1k-0 calls a mountain thorpe "A port. The wharves are…", a coherence defect outside this section.
8. PLAUSIBLE: the Foundry `&#39;` / `&amp;` / `\(` text is deliberate escaping that renders once Foundry converts it to HTML, but it leaks in the raw journal text. The Services list printing all 11 categories (Magic, Criminal) looks like `available` holding truthy objects for every key.
9. The owner's lines are counted PASS and not rewritten: "State, never fate" (HOUSE_MOTTO, Cover) and "Free preview. Forge your own at settlementforge.com". "Elder Council governs." (powerHeadline) passes; its "Military/Guard pressing close behind" is the faction-label leak noted in note 4.
10. The ECHO_PREFIXES glue in historyGenerator turns present-tense rows into impossible past ("…from that period reveal an innocent person … is about to be executed"). The pools need a past-tense twin for each row, not a prefix.

## §3 THE DUPLICATE-SURFACE MAP

Measured on the 12 (a sentence of ≥8 words printed ≥3 times inside ONE export of ONE dossier): **the screen is nearly clean — 5 sentences in 12 dossiers; the PDF is not — 61 sentences.** The screen, the PDF and the Foundry journal are three exports, so one sentence on each of the three is a mirror, not a duplicate; the defect is a sentence printed several times INSIDE one export.

| sentence (thorp unless named) | where it prints inside the PDF (×) | keep it on | elsewhere, instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| "{settlement} is receiving more people than its infrastructure was built for. New arrivals come faster…" (the stress summary) | cover, Overview "Active crises", Tonight crisis card, Defense "Active military status" (×4; screen: Defense ×1) | **the Tonight crisis card** (the PDF's crisis page) | cover: the one-line headline (§2a #1) only · Overview: the crisis LABEL and its Tonight page number · Defense: one line of what the crisis does to the defence ("half the muster is out finding food for the newcomers"; `needs` a defence-angle clause per stress type) |
| "In the migration, the brunt falls on {npc}…" and every Tonight hook | Plot Hooks, Tonight (×2 per hook) | **Plot Hooks** (the full list) | Tonight: the hook's TITLE and its number in chapter 04 |
| "Mass migration: an influx of newcomers stresses the food balance…" | Overview tensions, History tensions, Viability "Active stress effects" (×3; screen ×3: Overview, History, Viability) | **Economics › Food security** (it is a food fact) | tensions: the tension's own people line (§2a #19); Viability: the verdict's plain clause (§2a #4) |
| tension descriptions ("The supply of wool…", "The population changed faster…", "A surge in criminal activity…", "News of approaching danger…") | Overview, Plot Hooks "Underlying tensions", History "Live tensions" (×3, in 9 of 12 dossiers) | **History › Live tensions** | Overview: label + parties; Plot Hooks: nothing (the heading stays, the hooks under it are the content) |
| "{faction}: split. no direct stake; it could go either way." | Tonight "Stakes", one row per untouched house (×4–×6 in the town/city PDFs) | — | one line: "The {houses} have no stake in it and could go either way." (one sentence for all untouched houses) |
| "Shaped by events that left no monuments…" (historical character) | Overview "Character & origin", History lead, History header (×3; screen ×2) | **History lead** | Overview: the origin line only |
| "{a}'s relationship with {b} is more complicated…" + its evidence line | Overview "Notable connection", Relationships "Prominent", Relationships list (×3; the evidence line ×3) | **Relationships** | Overview: the names and the tie label ("Incitatus Varro & Walpurga Corvinus · Mutual Leverage") |
| "VIABLE BUT STRAINED: historically plausible…" | Viability headline, Viability verdict (×2; Foundry ×2) | the verdict | headline: §2a #4 |
| screen: "Contributes to Entrepôt Trade - 11% of income" (Portaminus) | screen Economics flows ×6 | — | machinery-leak: the chain card's income share is a number in the card, not a sentence |
| screen: "7 institutions operating on stockpiles only (isolated trade): …" (`soak1k-9`) | screen ×3 | the Economics warning | elsewhere nothing |

## §4 WHAT IS NOT REWRITTEN, AND WHY (the models)

These already carry the law; they are the measure the lanes write to.

- **"The road to Albamunicipium has acquired a verge of graves: recent, orderly, unmarked but tended. People arrived here in numbers, and not all of them finished the journey."** — evidence first (graves a traveller passes), then the plain fact, then the human cost; nothing names the mechanism.
- **"A benefactor's name is carved fresh above a mended well, and people make sure you notice it."** — a thing seen, and a behaviour that tells the DM who wants credit.
- **"Prices chalked outside Al-karez's market gate have been rubbed out and rewritten so often the board has gone grey. The numbers on it now are polite fictions; the real trading happens in back rooms, in kind."** — the famine shown by a board, not a percentage.
- **"Festival banners hang above the doors, faded from one year and waiting for the next."** (Copanucan) — plain, concrete, carries the calendar without saying "calendar".
- **"Every season brings more households to Albamunicipium and no more work. The newcomers camp where they can, and Elder Council is counting heads it has no plan for."** — the owner's own shape: what happened, to whom, what it costs, what is coming.
- **"A farmstead three miles out was destroyed last night. The farmer and his family are unaccounted for. The garrison is not going out to look."** (Portaminus) — three plain sentences, a person, a consequence, a refusal a party can act on.
- **"An occupying officer arrested a local elder for 'seditious speech'. Elected Reeve filed a formal protest. The protest was returned unread."** — event, response, answer; the DM can retell it word for word.
- **"Strangers in foreign colours stand where the roads meet in Copanucan, and the locals walk around them as if they were furniture nobody wanted."** — the one "stranger" line that works: the strangers ARE the evidence.
- **The NPC hooks** ("They buried someone last season that they refer to only as 'the traveller'. They will not say more.") — concrete and playable; their defect is repetition across towns (§2b hooks), not voice.
- **The institution identity texts** ("A corner of the holding left out of the plough, banked with turf and marked with fieldstones…") — catalogue text, audited for machinery only, and they pass.
- **The labels and characteristics** (Prosperity: Struggling, Safety: Tense, the readiness bars) — out of scope by the brief and by the owner's word; the prose above is written to sit beside them, not replace them.
